<!-- assumption-1: body_md -->
\label{ass:main}
    The set $\mathcal{X} \subseteq \mathbb{R}^d$ is non-empty, closed and convex, the function $f: \mathbb{R}^d \to \mathbb{R}$ is convex and differentiable, and the solution set $\mathcal{X}_\star \coloneqq \mathop{\arg\!\min}_{z \in \mathcal{X}} f(z)$ is non-empty.
<!-- end assumption-1 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{thm:lmo_is_projection}
    Fix $x \in \mathcal{X} \setminus \mathcal{X}_\star$ and an admissible radius $t \in (0, t(x)]$, and let $x_{\rm lmo} \in \mathop{\arg\!\min}_{z \in \mathcal{X} \cap\mathcal{B}(x,t)} \left\langle\nabla f(x), z\right\rangle$ be a {\color{gray}

\sfLocal LMO}\xspace step.
    Then:
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item \label{it:lmoproj-localizes}
        The {\color{gray}

\sfLocal LMO}\xspace depth is admissible and does not exceed the radius, that is,
        \begin{equation*}
            0 \ < \ s_{\rm lmo}(x,t) \ \le \ t \ \le \ t(x) \ \le \ s_\star(x) ,
        \end{equation*}
        so the half-space $\mathcal{H}_x\left(\nabla f(x), s_{\rm lmo}(x,t)\right)$ separates $x$ from, and localizes, the set of minimizers:
        \begin{equation*}
            x \notin \mathcal{H}_x\left(\nabla f(x), s_{\rm lmo}(x,t)\right) , \qquad \mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{H}_x\left(\nabla f(x), s_{\rm lmo}(x,t)\right) .
        \end{equation*}

        \item \label{it:lmoproj-projects}
        The projection onto the localized feasible set \textup{(\ref{eq:proj_family})} coincides with the {\color{gray}

  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\sfLocal LMO}\xspace step, that is, 
        \begin{equation*}
            x_{\rm lmo} = {\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}_x(\nabla f(x),\, s_{\rm lmo}(x,t))}(x) .
        \end{equation*}
        In particular, $\mathop{\arg\!\min}_{z \in \mathcal{X} \cap\mathcal{B}(x,t)} \left\langle\nabla f(x), z\right\rangle$ is a singleton.

        \item \label{it:lmoproj-stepsize}
        The method takes a step of size $t$, that is,
        \begin{equation*}
            {\left\| x_{\rm lmo} - x \right\|} = \operatorname{dist}\left(x, \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}_x(\nabla f(x),\, s_{\rm lmo}(x,t))\right) = t.
        \end{equation*}

        \item \label{it:lmoproj-distance}
        The squared distance of the new point to any minimizer is reduced by at least $t^2$, that is,
        \begin{equation*}
            {\left\| x_{\rm lmo} - x_\star \right\|}^2 \le {\left\| x - x_\star \right\|}^2 - t^2 , \quad \forall x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star .
        \end{equation*}
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
We write $N_\mathcal{C}(z)$ for the normal cone of a convex set $\mathcal{C}$ at a point $z \in \mathcal{C}$.
    The first-order optimality condition for the local subproblem \textup{(\ref{eq:local_lmo})} may be restated as $0 \in \nabla f(x)+N_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{\rm lmo})+N_{\mathcal{B}(x,t)}(x_{\rm lmo})$. 
    Since the normal cone of a ball is $\left\{0\right\}$ at an interior point and the ray through $x_{\rm lmo}-x$ at a boundary point (\Cref{lem:normal_cone_ball}), there exist $n \in N_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{\rm lmo})$ and $\lambda \ge 0$ with
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:sketch_kkt}
        \nabla f(x)+n+\lambda\left(x_{\rm lmo}-x\right) \ = \ 0 .
    \end{equation}
    Parts~\ref{it:lmoproj-stepsize} and~\ref{it:lmoproj-projects} both follow from \textup{(\ref{eq:sketch_kkt})}, in that order, each by a case split on whether the multiplier $\lambda$ vanishes; 
    parts~\ref{it:lmoproj-localizes} and~\ref{it:lmoproj-distance} are then consequences.
    Admissibility is used in the form
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:sketch_admissible}
        t \ \le \ t(x) \ = \ \frac{f(x)-f_\star}{{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}} \ \le \ \left\langle\frac{\nabla f(x)}{{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}}, x-x_\star\right\rangle , \qquad \forall x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star ,
    \end{equation}
    which is first-order convexity divided by ${\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}$.

    Suppose $\lambda > 0$. 
    Since the step is non-zero (\Cref{lem:local_fw_gap}), $\lambda(x_{\rm lmo}-x)$ is a non-zero element of $N_{\mathcal{B}(x,t)}(x_{\rm lmo})$, and that cone is $\left\{0\right\}$ at every interior point of the ball (\Cref{lem:normal_cone_ball}).
    Hence, $x_{\rm lmo}$ lies on the sphere and the step has length $t$. 
    If instead $\lambda = 0$, then $-\nabla f(x) \in N_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{\rm lmo})$, so $\left\langle\nabla f(x), x_{\rm lmo}-x_\star\right\rangle \le 0$ for every minimizer $x_\star$, while \textup{(\ref{eq:sketch_admissible})}, Cauchy--Schwarz and ${\left\| x_{\rm lmo}-x \right\|} \le t$ give the reverse inequality. 
    The resulting equality forces equality both in the Cauchy--Schwarz bound $\left\langle\nabla f(x), x_{\rm lmo}-x\right\rangle \ge -{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}\,{\left\| x_{\rm lmo}-x \right\|}$ and in ${\left\| x_{\rm lmo}-x \right\|} \le t$, so that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:sketch_antiparallel}
        x-x_{\rm lmo} \ = \ t\,\frac{\nabla f(x)}{{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}} , \qquad\text{and in particular}\qquad {\left\| x_{\rm lmo}-x \right\|} \ = \ t ,
    \end{equation}
    and the step has again length $t$, establishing part~\ref{it:lmoproj-stepsize}.

    For part~\ref{it:lmoproj-projects}, we must establish $\left\langlex-x_{\rm lmo}, y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle \le 0$ for every $y$ in the localized feasible set, this being the variational characterization of the projection. 
    First, $\left\langlen, y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle \le 0$, because $y \in \mathcal{X}$ and $n \in N_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{\rm lmo})$. 
    Second,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:sketch_membership}
        y \in \mathcal{H}_x\left(\nabla f(x),\, s_{\rm lmo}(x,t)\right) \qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad \left\langle\nabla f(x), y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle \ \le \ 0 ,
    \end{equation}
    since inserting the depth \textup{(\ref{def:s-lmo})} into the definition \textup{(\ref{eq:halfspace})} of the half-space reads $\left\langle\nabla f(x), x-y\right\rangle \ge \left\langle\nabla f(x), x-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle$.
    For $\lambda > 0$, taking the inner product of \textup{(\ref{eq:sketch_kkt})} with $y-x_{\rm lmo}$ and discarding the non-positive terms leaves $\lambda\left\langlex_{\rm lmo}-x, y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle \ge 0$, which is the claim. 
    For $\lambda = 0$, \textup{(\ref{eq:sketch_antiparallel})} gives $x-x_{\rm lmo}$ as a positive multiple of $\nabla f(x)$, and the claim is then \textup{(\ref{eq:sketch_membership})} again.

    In part~\ref{it:lmoproj-localizes}, $s_{\rm lmo}(x,t) \le t$ is Cauchy--Schwarz applied to the step $x-x_{\rm lmo}$, whose length is $t$ by part~\ref{it:lmoproj-stepsize}, and $s_{\rm lmo}(x,t) > 0$ holds because the feasible point $(1-\mu)x+\mu x_\star$ with $\mu = t/{\left\| x-x_\star \right\|}$ strictly improves the linear model (\Cref{lem:local_fw_gap}).
    Taking the infimum over $x_\star$ in \textup{(\ref{eq:sketch_admissible})} gives the remaining inequalities $t \le t(x) \le s_\star(x)$, which is what makes the cut localize $\mathcal{X}_\star$ (\Cref{thm:alpha_star_exact}).
    Part~\ref{it:lmoproj-distance} is the projection inequality of part~\ref{it:lmoproj-projects} evaluated at a minimizer, which lies in the localized feasible set by part~\ref{it:lmoproj-localizes}, together with the step length (\Cref{thm:projection_fejer_halfspace}).
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{thm:universal_band}
    Assume $\nabla f$ is Hölder continuous with exponent $\vartheta\in[0,1]$ and constant $L_\vartheta$ as in \textup{(\ref{eq:holder})}. 
    Let $\{x_k\}$ be generated from $x_0\in\mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}_\star$ by $x_{k+1} = {\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}_{x_k}(\nabla f(x_k),\, s_k)}(x_k)$ with any (possibly adaptive) depths in the deep-cut band, that is, satisfying $t(x_k) \le s_k \le s_\star(x_k)$ for all $k$, the run stopping if some $x_k \in \mathcal{X}_\star$.
    Then, for every $K \ge 1$, we have
    \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:band_rate}
        \min_{0\le k\le K} \bigl(f(x_k)-f_\star\bigr) \le \frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta} \left(\frac{\operatorname{dist}^2(x_0,\mathcal{X}_\star)}{K}\right)^{\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}} .
    \end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
The argument rests on three parts, and the Hölder data enter only in the last.
    First, cutting at least as deep as the Polyak cut:
    If $x_{k+1}$ lies in the Polyak cut at $x_k$, then the linearization of $f$ at $x_k$, evaluated at $x_{k+1}$, is at most $f_\star$. 
    Subtracting it from $f(x_{k+1})$ leaves
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:sketch_polyak_cut}
        f(x_{k+1})-f_\star \ \le \ D_f(x_{k+1},x_k) ,
    \end{equation}
    so the function gap may be upper-bounded by the Bregman divergence of a single step (\Cref{lem:polyak_cut}).
    Second, Fej\'er monotonicity (\Cref{thm:projection_fejer_halfspace}): 
    any cut retaining $\mathcal{X}_\star$ gives ${\left\| x_{k+1}-x_\star \right\|}^2 \le {\left\| x_k-x_\star \right\|}^2-{\left\| x_{k+1}-x_k \right\|}^2$, so the squared step lengths sum to at most $R^2$ and the shortest of $K$ steps obeys $\min_{k<K}{\left\| x_{k+1}-x_k \right\|}^2 \le R^2/K$.
    Third, Hölder continuity is invoked once, at that short step: 
    $D_f(x_{k+1},x_k) \le \tfrac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}{\left\| x_{k+1}-x_k \right\|}^{1+\vartheta}$. 
    Combining the three gives \textup{(\ref{eq:band_rate})}.
    Neither $\vartheta$ nor $L_\vartheta$ is used to generate the iterates; 
    they appear only in this last line, which is what makes the rate universal.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:localrate}
    Let $\nabla f$ be Hölder continuous with exponent $\vartheta \in [0,1]$ and constant $L_\vartheta$, and let $\{x_k\}$ be generated by {\color{gray}

\@ixpt\@xpt

  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\sfLocal LMO}\xspace at the Polyak radius $t_k=t(x_k)$ from $x_0 \in \mathcal{X} \setminus \mathcal{X}_\star$, the run stopping if some $x_k \in \mathcal{X}_\star$.
    Then, for all $K \ge 1$, we have
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:localrate}
        \min_{0\le k\le K}\bigl(f(x_k)-f_\star\bigr)\ \le\
        \max\left\{\ \Lambda_1\,K^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}}\ ,\ \ \Lambda_2\,K^{-\frac{1}{2-\vartheta}}\ \right\},
    \end{equation}
    where $R = \operatorname{dist}(x_0,\mathcal{X}_\star)$ and $G_\star = {\left\| \nabla f(x_\star) \right\|}$ for any $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$, and
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:lambdas}
        \Lambda_1\coloneqq \tfrac{25}{2}\,L_\vartheta R^{1+\vartheta},
        \qquad
        \Lambda_2\coloneqq 25 \max\Bigl\{\bigl(L_\vartheta R^2 G_\star^{1-\vartheta}\bigr)^{\frac1{2-\vartheta}},\ G_\star R\Bigr\} .
    \end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-3 -->

\sfLocal LMO}\xspace cut is no deeper than the Polyak cut, so $x_{k+1}$ need not lie in the latter and \textup{(\ref{eq:sketch_polyak_cut})} may fail. 
    The shortfall in depth, weighted by the gradient norm, is what we call the overshoot,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{def:overshoot}
        \Gamma_k \ \coloneqq \ {\left\| g_k \right\|}\left(t(x_k)-s_{\rm lmo}(x_k,t_k)\right) \ \ge \ 0 ,
    \end{equation}
    which vanishes precisely when the {\color{gray}

  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize\small\@ixpt\@xpt

\sfLocal LMO}\xspace cut is the Polyak cut (\Cref{lem:overshoot}).
    The exact identity $f(x_{k+1})-f_\star = D_f(x_{k+1},x_k) + \Gamma_k$ shows that the amount by which \textup{(\ref{eq:sketch_polyak_cut})} fails is exactly the overshoot. 
    Since $D_f \ge 0$, no {\color{gray}

  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize\small\@ixpt\@xpt

\@ixpt\@xpt

\@ixpt\@xpt

  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip

\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\sfLocal LMO}\xspace step can push the suboptimality below $\Gamma_k$, however smooth $f$ may be (\Cref{lem:overshoot_lower_bound}).

    The quantity that our analysis rests on is, with $\ell_k \coloneqq \left\langleg_k, x_k-x_{k+1}\right\rangle$ the decrease of the linear model,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{def:rho}
        \rho_k \ \coloneqq \ \frac{\ell_k}{a_k} \ \in \ (0,1] ,
    \end{equation}
    the fraction of the first-order decrease available at $x_k$ that the oracle actually captures; 
    since $\ell_k = {\left\| g_k \right\|}s_{\rm lmo}(x_k,t_k)$ and $a_k = {\left\| g_k \right\|}t(x_k)$, the range $(0,1]$ is part~\ref{it:lmoproj-localizes} of \Cref{thm:lmo_is_projection} and $\Gamma_k = (1-\rho_k)a_k$.
    At the Polyak radius, $\rho_k$ is the cosine of the angle between $g_k$ and the step $x_k-x_{k+1}$: 
    it equals $1$ when the step runs straight down the gradient and tends to $0$ when the feasible set forces the step almost orthogonal to $g_k$.

    A small $\rho_k$ is bad for the function value---the floor $\Gamma_k$ leaves $a_{k+1} \ge (1-\rho_k)a_k$, so one step removes at most a $\rho_k$ fraction of the gap, while the descent inequality delivers only $a_{k+1} \le a_k(1-\rho_k+\delta_k)$, with $\delta_k \coloneqq \tfrac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}t_k^{1+\vartheta}/a_k$ the Hölder error---but it is good for the distance.
    The projection identity of \Cref{thm:lmo_is_projection} sharpens Fej\'er monotonicity to
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:sketch_sharp_fejer}
        {\left\| x_{k+1}-x_\star \right\|}^2 \ \le \ {\left\| x_k-x_\star \right\|}^2-t_k^2\,\frac{2-\rho_k}{\rho_k}
    \end{equation}
    (\Cref{thm:sharp_fejer}), in which the overshoot amplifies the contraction by the factor $(2-\rho_k)/\rho_k \ge 1/\rho_k$: a nearly tangential step barely lowers $f$, but pulls the iterate closer to $\mathcal{X}_\star$ than its length alone would suggest.
    Summing gives the refined budget $\sum_{k<K}t_k^2/\rho_k \le R^2$.
    The two fit together because
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:sketch_gaincost}
        \underbrace{\frac{\rho_k}{a_k^2}}_{\text{gain in }\Phi_k \coloneqq a_k^{-2}} \cdot \underbrace{\frac{t_k^2}{\rho_k}}_{\text{Fej\'er cost}} \ = \ \frac{t_k^2}{a_k^2} \ = \ \frac{1}{{\left\| g_k \right\|}^2}
    \end{equation}
    does not depend on $\rho_k$ at all: 
    a step that makes little progress in function value pays for it in the Fej\'er budget by exactly the reciprocal factor, so no choice of $\rho_k$ is free.
    
    The remainder of the proof rests on an accounting argument.
    We write $\bar a$ for the level below which $G_\star$ dominates the Hölder part of the gradient bound; 
    above it the refined budget alone delivers the exponent $\tfrac{1+\vartheta}{2}$.
    Below $\bar a$, a two-sided gradient bound pins ${\left\| g_k \right\|}$ to within a constant factor of $G_\star$, and the $K$ steps are counted in three groups: 
    those above $\bar a$ are few by the budget; 
    those that stall, in the sense $\rho_k < 2\delta_k$, are few because each consumes a fixed share of the budget; 
    and the productive remainder is counted by Cauchy--Schwarz against $\Phi_k$ using \textup{(\ref{eq:sketch_gaincost})}, a count needed only once $K$ is large. 
    This produces the exponent $\tfrac1{2-\vartheta}$, and \textup{(\ref{eq:localrate})} is the larger of the two bounds.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- head-1: body_md -->
\Cref{thm:lmo_is_projection} establishes that the {\color{gray}

\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\sfLocal LMO}\xspace step is the projection of $x$ onto $\mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x), \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t))$, that its length is exactly $t$, and that the squared distance to any minimizer decreases by at least $t^2$.

Our proof of this result takes the following form.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item    
    \Cref{lem:zero_grad,lem:generic-decomposition,lem:normal_cone_ball} establish basic geometric properties that are used throughout this and the following sections.

    \item
    \Cref{thm:alpha_star_exact} shows that the set of minimizers, $\mathcal{X}_\star$, lies in every sufficiently shallow half-space $\mathcal{H} = \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha)$ with $\alpha \ge \alpha_\star(x)$.
    From this, we establish Fejér monotonicity, a key ingredient for the remaining proof steps, for the whole family of projection methods of the form \textup{(\ref{eq:proj_family})} in \Cref{thm:projection_fejer_halfspace}.

    \item
    \Cref{thm:alpha_star_exact,lem:alpha_t_vs_alpha_cvx,lem:alpha_t_le_alpha_lmo} show that $\alpha_\star(x) \le \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) \le \alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t) \le \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t)$ for admissible $t$, with $\alpha_{\rm cvx} = \alpha_{\rm proj}$ when $t = t(x)$.

    \item
    \Cref{thm:lmo_boundary_and_projection} shows that the linear minimizer over $\mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{B}(x,t)$ lies on the sphere ${\left\| z-x \right\|} = t$, and that it satisfies the variational characterization of the projection of $x$ onto $\mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t))$; that the distance from $x$ to this set equals $t$ is then a consequence.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end head-1 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:zero_grad}
    If $x \in \mathcal{X} \setminus \mathcal{X}_\star$, then $\nabla f(x) \neq 0$.
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
We show the contrapositive.
    Suppose $x \in \mathcal{X}$ and $\nabla f(x) = 0$.
    Then, by convexity and differentiability of $f$ (\Cref{ass:main}), we have
    \begin{equation*}
        f(y) \ge f(x) + \left\langle\nabla f(x), y-x\right\rangle = f(x), \qquad \forall y \in \mathbb{R}^d.
    \end{equation*}
    Thus, $x$ is a global minimizer of $f$, and, in particular, a minimizer over the feasible set $\mathcal{X}$.
    Hence, $x \in \mathcal{X}_\star$.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{lem:grad_const_on_Xstar}
    The gradient of $f$ is constant on the solution set: $\nabla f(u) = \nabla f(v)$ for all $u,v \in \mathcal{X}_\star$.
    In particular, $G_\star \coloneqq {\left\| \nabla f(x_\star) \right\|}$ does not depend on the choice of $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Let $u,v \in \mathcal{X}_\star$.
    First-order optimality of $v$ for $f$ over $\mathcal{X}$ gives $\left\langle\nabla f(v), u-v\right\rangle \ge 0$, while $f(u) = f(v) = f_\star$, so
    \begin{equation*}
        D_f(u,v) \ = \ f(u)-f(v)-\left\langle\nabla f(v), u-v\right\rangle \ = \ -\left\langle\nabla f(v), u-v\right\rangle \ \le \ 0 .
    \end{equation*}
    Convexity of $f$ gives $D_f(u,v) \ge 0$, hence $D_f(u,v) = 0$, that is $\left\langle\nabla f(v), u-v\right\rangle = 0$.
    Therefore, for every $w \in \mathbb{R}^d$, convexity at $v$ yields
    \begin{equation*}
        f(w) \ \ge \ f(v)+\left\langle\nabla f(v), w-v\right\rangle \ = \ f(u)+\left\langle\nabla f(v), w-u\right\rangle ,
    \end{equation*}
    the last equality because $f(v) = f(u)$ and $\left\langle\nabla f(v), u-v\right\rangle = 0$.
    This is the subgradient inequality for $\nabla f(v)$ at $u$, so $\nabla f(v) \in \partial f(u) = \left\{\nabla f(u)\right\}$, the last set equality because $f$ is differentiable \citep[Theorem~25.1]{rockafellar1997convex}.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{lem:generic-decomposition}
    For any three points $x_{+}, x, z \in \mathbb{R}^d$,
    \begin{equation*}
        {\left\| x_{+} - z \right\|}^2 = {\left\|  x - z \right\|}^2 - 2\left\langlex - x_{+}, x_{+} - z\right\rangle -{\left\| x - x_{+} \right\|}^2.
    \end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
We write $x-z = (x-x_{+})+(x_{+}-z)$. 
    Hence,
    \begin{equation*}
        {\left\| x-z \right\|}^2 = {\left\| (x-x_{+}) + (x_{+}-z) \right\|}^2 = {\left\| x-x_{+} \right\|}^2 + {\left\| x_{+}-z \right\|}^2 + 2\left\langlex-x_{+}, x_{+}-z\right\rangle.
    \end{equation*}
    Rearranging this identity yields
    \begin{equation*}
        {\left\| x_{+}-z \right\|}^2 = {\left\| x-z \right\|}^2 - 2\left\langlex - x_{+}, x_{+} - z\right\rangle -{\left\| x - x_{+} \right\|}^2.
    \end{equation*}
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{lem:normal_cone_ball}
    Let $x \in \mathbb{R}^d$ and $t > 0$, and define the closed Euclidean ball
    \begin{equation*}
        \mathcal{B}(x,t) \coloneqq \left\{z\in \mathbb{R}^d \;:\; {\left\| z-x \right\|}\le t\right\}.
    \end{equation*}
    Then, for any $y \in \mathbb{R}^d$, the normal cone of $\mathcal{B}(x,t)$ at $y$ is given by
    \begin{equation*}
        N_{\mathcal{B}(x,t)}(y) =
        \begin{cases}
        \emptyset, & y \notin \mathcal{B}(x,t),\\[1ex]
        \left\{0\right\}, & {\left\| y-x \right\|} < t,\\[1ex]
        \left\{\lambda (y-x)\;:\; \lambda\ge 0\right\}, & {\left\| y-x \right\|} = t.
        \end{cases}
    \end{equation*}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Recall that, for a convex set $\mathcal{C}$ and $y \in \mathcal{C}$, $N_\mathcal{C}(y) = \left\{v \in \mathbb{R}^d \;:\; \left\langlev, z-y\right\rangle \le 0 \ \forall z \in \mathcal{C}\right\}$, while $N_\mathcal{C}(y) = \emptyset$ for $y \notin \mathcal{C}$.
    This settles the case $y \notin \mathcal{B}(x,t)$. 

    For the remainder of this proof, we write $\mathcal{B} \coloneqq \mathcal{B}(x,t)$.

    \emph{Case ${\left\| y-x \right\|} < t$.}
    Clearly $0 \in N_\mathcal{B}(y)$. 
    Let $v \in N_\mathcal{B}(y)$ and suppose $v \neq 0$.
    Set $\varepsilon \coloneqq t - {\left\| y-x \right\|} > 0$ and $z \coloneqq y + \varepsilon v/{\left\| v \right\|}$.
    Then ${\left\| z-x \right\|} \le {\left\| y-x \right\|} + \varepsilon = t$, so $z \in \mathcal{B}$, but $\left\langlev, z-y\right\rangle = \varepsilon{\left\| v \right\|} > 0$, a contradiction.
    Hence $N_\mathcal{B}(y) = \left\{0\right\}$.

    \emph{Case ${\left\| y-x \right\|} = t$.}
    Let $\lambda \ge 0$ and $z \in \mathcal{B}$. 
    By Cauchy--Schwarz,
    \begin{align*}
        \left\langle\lambda(y-x), z-y\right\rangle & = \lambda\left(\left\langley-x, z-x\right\rangle - {\left\| y-x \right\|}^2\right) \\
        & \le \lambda\left({\left\| y-x \right\|}{\left\| z-x \right\|} - t^2\right) \\
        & \le \lambda\left(t^2 - t^2\right) = 0 ,        
    \end{align*}
    so $\lambda(y-x) \in N_\mathcal{B}(y)$.
    
    Next, let $v \in N_\mathcal{B}(y)$, set $u \coloneqq (y-x)/t$ (a unit vector), and decompose $v = \mu u + w$ with $\mu \coloneqq \left\langlev, u\right\rangle$ and $\left\langlew, u\right\rangle = 0$.
    Choosing $z = x \in \mathcal{B}$ gives $0 \ge \left\langlev, x-y\right\rangle = -t\mu$, so $\mu \ge 0$.
    Suppose $w \neq 0$, let $\hat w \coloneqq w/{\left\| w \right\|}$, and for $\beta \in (0,\pi/2]$ consider $z_\beta \coloneqq x + t\left(\cos\beta\, u + \sin\beta\, \hat w\right)$.
    Since $u \perp \hat w$ are unit vectors, ${\left\| z_\beta - x \right\|} = t$, so $z_\beta \in \mathcal{B}$.
    Using $z_\beta - y = t\left((\cos\beta - 1)u + \sin\beta\, \hat w\right)$, $1-\cos\beta \le \beta^2/2$ and $\sin\beta \ge 2\beta/\pi$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langlev, z_\beta - y\right\rangle = t\left(\mu(\cos\beta-1) + {\left\| w \right\|}\sin\beta\right) \ge t\beta\left(\tfrac{2}{\pi}{\left\| w \right\|} - \tfrac{\mu}{2}\beta\right) \ ,
    \end{equation*}
    which is positive for all sufficiently small $\beta > 0$, contradicting $v \in N_\mathcal{B}(y)$.
    Therefore $w = 0$ and $v = \mu u = (\mu/t)(y-x)$ with $\mu/t \ge 0$.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:alpha_star_exact}
    Fix a point $x \in \mathbb{R}^d$.
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item
        The quantity
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:alpha_star_only_def}
            \alpha_\star(x) \coloneqq \sup_{x_\star\in \mathcal{X}_\star}\left\langle\nabla f(x), x_\star\right\rangle
        \end{equation}
        is finite.
        In particular,
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:alpha_star_le_alpha_cvx_1}
            \alpha_\star(x) \leq \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x),
        \end{equation}
        where
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:alpha_cvx_def}
            \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) \coloneqq \left\langle\nabla f(x), x\right\rangle - \left(f(x)-f_\star\right) < +\infty.
        \end{equation}
        \item
        For $\alpha \in \mathbb{R}$, the following statements are equivalent:
        \begin{enumerate}
            \item
            $\mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha)$,

            \item
            $\left\langle\nabla f(x), x_\star\right\rangle \le \alpha$ for every $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$,
            
            \item
            $\alpha_\star(x) \le \alpha$.
        \end{enumerate}
        \item
        The following inclusions hold:
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:alpha_star}
            \mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_\star(x)) \subseteq \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x)).
        \end{equation}
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
\begin{enumerate}
        \item
        Let $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$ be arbitrary.
        Since $f$ is convex and differentiable on $\mathbb{R}^d$ (\Cref{ass:main}), the first-order convexity inequality gives
        \begin{equation*}
            f_\star = f(x_\star) \ge f(x)+\left\langle\nabla f(x), x_\star-x\right\rangle .
        \end{equation*}
        Rearranging, we obtain
        \begin{equation*}
            \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_\star\right\rangle \le \left\langle\nabla f(x), x\right\rangle - \left(f(x)-f_\star\right) = \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) < + \infty.
        \end{equation*}
        Since this inequality holds for every $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$, we may take the supremum over $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$ and get
        \begin{equation*}
            \alpha_\star(x) = \sup_{x_\star\in \mathcal{X}_\star}\left\langle\nabla f(x), x_\star\right\rangle \le \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) < +\infty.
        \end{equation*}
        Moreover, since $\mathcal{X}_\star \neq \emptyset$ (\Cref{ass:main}) the supremum is over a non-empty set, hence $\alpha_\star(x) >-\infty$.
        This proves \textup{(\ref{eq:alpha_star_le_alpha_cvx_1})}.
        \item
        Let $g = \nabla f(x)$.
        By definition of $\mathcal{H}(g,\alpha)$, for any $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$,
        \begin{equation*}
            x_\star \in \mathcal{H}(g,\alpha) \qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad \left\langleg, x_\star\right\rangle \le \alpha.
        \end{equation*}
        Therefore,
        \begin{equation*}
            \mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{H}(g,\alpha) \qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad \left\langleg, x_\star\right\rangle \le \alpha \quad \forall x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star.
        \end{equation*}
        This proves the equivalence of i. and ii.

        Next, statement ii. is equivalent to saying that $\alpha$ is an upper bound on the set
        \begin{equation*}
            \left\{\left\langleg, x_\star\right\rangle\;:\; x_\star\in \mathcal{X}_\star\right\}.
        \end{equation*}
        By the definition \textup{(\ref{eq:alpha_star_only_def})} of $\alpha_\star$, this is equivalent to
        \begin{equation*}
            \sup_{x_\star\in \mathcal{X}_\star}\left\langleg, x_\star\right\rangle \le \alpha,
        \end{equation*}
        that is, $\alpha_\star(x) \le \alpha$. 
        This proves the equivalence of ii. and iii.
        \item
        This follows immediately by combining part~2 and \textup{(\ref{eq:alpha_star_le_alpha_cvx_1})}, and using the definition \textup{(\ref{eq:halfspace_level})}.
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{thm:projection_fejer_halfspace}
    Fix $x_0 \in \mathcal{X}$, and define
    \begin{equation*}
        x_{k+1} \coloneqq {\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x_k),\alpha_k)}(x_k), \qquad k = 0,1,\dots,
    \end{equation*}
    where $\alpha_k \geq \alpha_\star(x_k)$.
    Then, for all $k \geq 0$, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:sq-distance-shrink}
        {\left\| x_{k+1} - x_{\star} \right\|}^2 \leq {\left\| x_k - x_{\star} \right\|}^2 - {\left\| x_k - x_{k+1} \right\|}^2 \qquad \forall x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star,
    \end{equation}
    and therefore, for all $K \geq 1$, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:min-sq-distance-bound}
        \min_{0\leq k \leq K-1} {\left\| x_k - x_{k+1} \right\|}^2 \leq \frac{\operatorname{dist}^2(x_0,\mathcal{X}_\star)}{K}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
By \Cref{lem:generic-decomposition}, we know that
    \begin{equation*}
        {\left\| x_{k+1} - x_\star \right\|}^2 = {\left\|  x_k - x_\star \right\|}^2 - 2\left\langlex_k - x_{k+1}, x_{k+1} - x_\star\right\rangle - {\left\| x_k - x_{k+1} \right\|}^2
    \end{equation*}
    for all $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$.
    Hence, we only need to show that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eqL:i-fd8798fgfg}
        \left\langlex_k - x_{k+1}, x_{k+1} - x_\star\right\rangle \ge 0.
    \end{equation}

    Let $g_k = \nabla f(x_k)$.
    Since $\alpha_k \geq \alpha_\star(x_k)$, \Cref{thm:alpha_star_exact} implies that $\mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_k)$.
    Since also $\mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{X}$, we have
    \begin{equation*}
        x_\star \in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_k), \qquad \forall x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star.
    \end{equation*}
    Because $x_{k+1}$ is the Euclidean projection of $x_k$ onto the non-empty closed convex set $\mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_k)$, the standard projection optimality condition yields
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langlex_k - x_{k+1}, y - x_{k+1}\right\rangle \le 0, \qquad \forall y \in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_k).
    \end{equation*}
    Since $\mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_k)$, the above inequality holds for all $y = x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$, establishing \textup{(\ref{eqL:i-fd8798fgfg})}.

    Finally, \textup{(\ref{eq:min-sq-distance-bound})} follows by summing up inequalities \textup{(\ref{eq:sq-distance-shrink})} for $k = 0,\dots,K-1$.
    Indeed, the inequalities
    \begin{align*}
        {\left\| x_1-x_\star \right\|}^2 & \leq {\left\| x_0-x_\star \right\|}^2 - {\left\| x_0-x_1 \right\|}^2\\
        {\left\| x_2-x_\star \right\|}^2 & \leq {\left\| x_1-x_\star \right\|}^2 - {\left\| x_1-x_2 \right\|}^2\\
        & \vdots \\
        {\left\| x_K-x_\star \right\|}^2 & \leq {\left\| x_{K-1}-x_\star \right\|}^2 - {\left\| x_{K-1}-x_K \right\|}^2
    \end{align*}
    add up to
    \begin{equation*}
        {\left\| x_K-x_\star \right\|}^2 \leq {\left\| x_0-x_\star \right\|}^2 - \sum_{k=0}^{K-1}{\left\| x_{k} - x_{k+1} \right\|}^2,
    \end{equation*}
    and after rearranging and dividing by $K$, we get
    \begin{equation*}
        \min_{0\leq k \leq K-1} {\left\| x_k - x_{k+1} \right\|}^2 \leq \frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}{\left\| x_{k} - x_{k+1} \right\|}^2 \leq \frac{{\left\| x_0-x_\star \right\|}^2 - {\left\| x_K-x_\star \right\|}^2}{K} \leq \frac{{\left\| x_0-x_\star \right\|}^2 }{K}.
    \end{equation*}
    It only remains to choose
    \begin{equation*}
        x_\star = \mathop{\arg\!\min}_{z \in \mathcal{X}_\star} {\left\| x_0 - z \right\|},
    \end{equation*}
    so that ${\left\| x_0-x_\star \right\|} = \operatorname{dist}(x_0,\mathcal{X}_\star)$.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:alpha_t_vs_alpha_cvx}
    Fix $x \in \mathbb{R}^d$ and assume $\nabla f(x) \neq 0$.
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item
        If $t \le t(x)$, then
        \begin{equation*}
            \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) \le \alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t).
        \end{equation*}
        
        \item
        If $t = t(x)$, then
        \begin{equation*}
            \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) = \alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t).
        \end{equation*}
    \end{enumerate}
    Consequently, if $t \le t(x)$, then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:alpha_t_cvx_inclusion}
        \mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x)) \subseteq \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t)).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
By definition, $\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) = \left\langle\nabla f(x), x\right\rangle-\left(f(x)-f_\star\right)$, while $\alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t) = \left\langle\nabla f(x), x\right\rangle-t{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}$. 
    Hence
    \begin{equation*}
        \alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t)-\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) = \left(f(x)-f_\star\right)-t{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}.
    \end{equation*}
    Using the definition \textup{(\ref{def:polyak-radius})} of $t(x)$, we can rewrite this as
    \begin{equation*}
        \alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t)-\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) = {\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}\left(t(x)-t\right).
    \end{equation*}
    Therefore, if $t \le t(x)$, then $\alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t)-\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) \ge 0,$ which proves $\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) \le \alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t)$. 
    If $t = t(x)$, then $\alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t)-\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) = 0$, and thus $\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) = \alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t).$
    The first inclusion in \textup{(\ref{eq:alpha_t_cvx_inclusion})} is \textup{(\ref{eq:alpha_star_le_alpha_cvx_1})} of \Cref{thm:alpha_star_exact} together with part~2 of that theorem; the second is $\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) \le \alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t)$ and the monotonicity of $\mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\cdot)$.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:alpha_t_le_alpha_lmo}
    Fix $x \in \mathcal{X} \setminus \mathcal{X}_\star$.
    For $t > 0$, define
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:alpha-lmo-def}
        \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t) \coloneqq \min_{z\in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{B}(x,t)} \left\langle\nabla f(x), z\right\rangle.
    \end{equation}    
    Then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:alpha_t_lmo}
        \alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t) \le \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t).
    \end{equation}
    Consequently, if $0 < t \le t(x)$, then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:alpha_t_lmo_inclusion}
        \mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t)) \subseteq \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t)).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Let $g = \nabla f(x)$, $\alpha_{\rm lmo} = \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t)$ and $\alpha_{\rm proj} = \alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t)$.

\bf} 
{Step 1: Non-zero gradient.}
    Since $x \notin \mathcal{X}_\star$, we have $g \neq 0$ (\Cref{lem:zero_grad}).

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Step 2: Inequality between the thresholds.}
    Fix any $z \in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{B}(x,t)$.
    Since $z \in \mathcal{B}(x,t)$, we have ${\left\| z-x \right\|} \le t$. 
    Therefore, by the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality,
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langleg, z\right\rangle = \left\langleg, x\right\rangle+\left\langleg, z-x\right\rangle \ge \left\langleg, x\right\rangle-{\left\| g \right\|}\,{\left\| z-x \right\|} \ge \left\langleg, x\right\rangle-t{\left\| g \right\|} = \alpha_{\rm proj}.
    \end{equation*}
    Since this holds for every $z \in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{B}(x,t)$, taking the minimum over $z \in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{B}(x,t)$ yields
    \begin{equation*}
        \alpha_{\rm lmo} = \min_{z\in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{B}(x,t)} \left\langleg, z\right\rangle \ge \alpha_{\rm proj}.
    \end{equation*}
    This proves \textup{(\ref{eq:alpha_t_lmo})}.

\bf} 
{Step 3: Relationship between the half-spaces.}
    Now assume in addition that $0 < t \le t(x)$.
    By \textup{(\ref{eq:alpha_t_cvx_inclusion})} of \Cref{lem:alpha_t_vs_alpha_cvx}, we have
    \begin{equation*}
        \mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{H}(g,\alpha_{\rm proj}).
    \end{equation*}
    Moreover, the inclusion
    \begin{equation*}
        \mathcal{H}(g,\alpha_{\rm proj}) \subseteq \mathcal{H}(g,\alpha_{\rm lmo})
    \end{equation*}
    follows from \textup{(\ref{eq:alpha_t_lmo})}.
    Combining the last two inclusions establishes \textup{(\ref{eq:alpha_t_lmo_inclusion})}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{thm:lmo_boundary_and_projection}
    Fix $x \in \mathcal{X} \setminus \mathcal{X}_\star$, choose
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:polyak_radius_bound_combined}
        0 < t \le t(x) \coloneqq \frac{f(x)-f_\star}{{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}},
    \end{equation}
    and let
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:lmo_step_combined}
        x_{\rm lmo} \in \mathop{\arg\!\min}_{z\in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{B}(x,t)} \left\langle\nabla f(x), z\right\rangle,
    \end{equation}
    with
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:alpha_lmo_combined}
        \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t) \coloneqq \min_{z\in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{B}(x,t)} \left\langle\nabla f(x), z\right\rangle = \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle .
    \end{equation}
    Then,
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item \label{it:bdry-sphere}
        $x_{\rm lmo}$ lies on the boundary of the ball $\mathcal{B}(x,t)$, that is,
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:lmo_step_has_length_t_combined}
            {\left\| x_{\rm lmo}-x \right\|} = t;
        \end{equation}

        \item \label{it:bdry-proj}
        $x_{\rm lmo}$ is the Euclidean projection of $x$ onto the intersection of $\mathcal{X}$ with the half-space
        \begin{equation*}
            \mathcal{H}_{\rm lmo} \coloneqq \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t)),
        \end{equation*}
        that is,
        \begin{equation}
            \label{eq:lmo_equals_projection_halfspace_combined}
            x_{\rm lmo} = {\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}_{\rm lmo}}(x).
        \end{equation}
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Let $g \coloneqq \nabla f(x)$.

\bf} 
{Step 1: Non-zero gradient.}
    Since $x \notin \mathcal{X}_\star$, we have $\nabla f(x) \neq 0$ (\Cref{lem:zero_grad}).

\bf} 
{Step 2: Non-zero step.}
    We now show that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:non_zero_step}
        x_{\rm lmo} \neq x,
    \end{equation}
    which will be useful later.

    Assume, for the sake of contradiction, that $x_{\rm lmo} = x$. 
    Since $x_{\rm lmo}$ solves \textup{(\ref{eq:lmo_step_combined})}, this implies that $x$ minimizes the linear functional
    \begin{equation*}
        z \mapsto \left\langle\nabla f(x), z\right\rangle
    \end{equation*}
    over $\mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{B}(x,t)$.
    Hence,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:optimality_x_itself_lemma}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), z-x\right\rangle \ge 0, \qquad \forall z \in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{B}(x,t).
    \end{equation}

    Now fix any $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$.
    Since $f$ is convex and differentiable (\Cref{ass:main}), we have
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:cvx_0978ttfyif}
        f_\star = f(x_\star) \ge f(x)+\left\langle\nabla f(x), x_\star-x\right\rangle.
    \end{equation}
    Since $x \notin \mathcal{X}_\star$, we have $f(x) > f_\star$, and therefore
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:strict_inner_positive_lemma}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), x-x_\star\right\rangle \overset{\textup{(\ref{eq:cvx_0978ttfyif})}}{\geq} f(x) - f_\star > 0.
    \end{equation}

    Because the radius $t$ satisfies \textup{(\ref{eq:polyak_radius_bound_combined})}, and
    \begin{equation*}
        f(x)-f_\star \overset{\textup{(\ref{eq:cvx_0978ttfyif})}}{\leq} \left\langle\nabla f(x), x-x_\star\right\rangle \le {\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}\,{\left\| x-x_\star \right\|},
    \end{equation*}
    it follows that $0 < t \le {\left\| x-x_\star \right\|}$. 
    The scalar $\lambda \coloneqq \frac{t}{{\left\| x-x_\star \right\|}}$ thus belongs to $(0,1]$.
    Define
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:0970fyd808fg}
        z_t \coloneqq (1-\lambda)x+\lambda x_\star = x-\frac{t}{{\left\| x-x_\star \right\|}}(x-x_\star).
    \end{equation}
    Since $x,x_\star \in \mathcal{X}$ and $\mathcal{X}$ is convex (\Cref{ass:main}), we have $z_t \in \mathcal{X}$ also. 
    Therefore,
    \begin{equation*}
        {\left\| z_t-x \right\|} \overset{\textup{(\ref{eq:0970fyd808fg})}}{=} \frac{t}{{\left\| x-x_\star \right\|}}{\left\| x-x_\star \right\|} = t.
    \end{equation*}
    Hence, $z_t \in \mathcal{B}(x,t)$, which implies that $z_t \in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{B}(x,t)$. 
    Applying \textup{(\ref{eq:optimality_x_itself_lemma})} with $z = z_t$, we obtain
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), z_t-x\right\rangle \ge 0.
    \end{equation*}
    On the other hand,
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), z_t-x\right\rangle \overset{\textup{(\ref{eq:0970fyd808fg})}}{=} -\frac{t}{{\left\| x-x_\star \right\|}}\left\langle\nabla f(x), x-x_\star\right\rangle.
    \end{equation*}
    By \textup{(\ref{eq:strict_inner_positive_lemma})}, the right-hand side is strictly negative, and hence
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), z_t-x\right\rangle < 0,
    \end{equation*}
    which contradicts inequality \textup{(\ref{eq:optimality_x_itself_lemma})}.
    We therefore conclude that $x_{\rm lmo} \neq x$.

\bf} 
{Step 3: An upper bound on the radius.}
    Fix any $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$.
    Since $f$ is convex and differentiable (\Cref{ass:main}),
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:h8fd+=-9=08fg}
        f_\star = f(x_\star) \ge f(x)+\left\langle\nabla f(x), x_\star-x\right\rangle.
    \end{equation}
    Combining this with \textup{(\ref{eq:polyak_radius_bound_combined})}, we get
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:combined_admissibility_corrected}
        0 < t \overset{\textup{(\ref{eq:polyak_radius_bound_combined})}}{\leq} t(x) \overset{\textup{(\ref{def:polyak-radius})}}{=} \frac{f(x)-f_\star}{{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}} \overset{\textup{(\ref{eq:h8fd+=-9=08fg})}}{\leq} \frac{\left\langle\nabla f(x), x-x_\star\right\rangle}{{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}}.
    \end{equation}

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@ \@plus 3\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{Step 4: Optimality conditions for the local linear minimization problem.}
    Since $x_{\rm lmo}$ solves the optimization problem
    \begin{equation*}
        \min_{z\in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{B}(x,t)} \left\langle\nabla f(x), z\right\rangle,
    \end{equation*}
    the first-order optimality condition \citep[Theorem~27.4]{rockafellar1997convex} gives $0 \in \nabla f(x)+N_{\mathcal{X} \cap\mathcal{B}(x,t)}(x_{\rm lmo})$, where $N_\mathcal{C}(z)$ denotes the normal cone of the set $\mathcal{C}$ at point $z$.
    The sum rule $N_{\mathcal{X} \cap\mathcal{B}(x,t)} = N_{\mathcal{X}}+N_{\mathcal{B}(x,t)}$ applies here as both sets are convex and their relative interiors meet \citep[Corollary~23.8.1]{rockafellar1997convex}.
    Indeed, $\operatorname{ri} \mathcal{B}(x,t) = \operatorname{int} \mathcal{B}(x,t) = \left\{z \;:\; {\left\| z-x \right\|} < t\right\}$ is an open set containing $x$.
    Since $\mathcal{X}$ is non-empty and convex, $x \in \mathcal{X} \subseteq \operatorname{cl} \mathcal{X} = \operatorname{cl}\left(\operatorname{ri} \mathcal{X}\right)$ \citep[Theorem~6.3]{rockafellar1997convex}, so this open neighborhood of $x$ contains a point of $\operatorname{ri}\mathcal{X}$, i.e. $\operatorname{ri}\mathcal{X} \cap \operatorname{ri}\mathcal{B}(x,t) \neq \emptyset$.

    \Cref{lem:normal_cone_ball} says that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:normal_cone_ball_xx}
        N_{\mathcal{B}(x,t)}(y) =
        \begin{cases}
        \emptyset, & y \notin \mathcal{B}(x,t),\\[1ex]
        \left\{0\right\}, & {\left\| y-x \right\|} < t,\\[1ex]
        \left\{\lambda' (y-x)\;:\; \lambda' \geq 0\right\}, & {\left\| y-x \right\|} = t.
        \end{cases}
    \end{equation}
    Since $x_{\rm lmo} \in \mathcal{B}(x,t)$, we conclude that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:-8-y8f9dufd}
        N_{\mathcal{B}(x,t)}(x_{\rm lmo}) = \begin{cases} \left\{0\right\}, & {\left\| x_{\rm lmo}-x \right\|} < t,\\[1ex]
        \left\{\lambda' (x_{\rm lmo}-x)\;:\; \lambda' \geq 0\right\}, & {\left\| x_{\rm lmo}-x \right\|} = t.
        \end{cases}
    \end{equation}
    Hence, there exist $n \in N_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{\rm lmo})$ and $\lambda \ge 0$ such that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:kkt_corrected}
        \nabla f(x) + n + \lambda(x_{\rm lmo}-x) = 0.
    \end{equation}

\bf} 
{Step 5: Moving to the boundary of the ball.}
    Claim \ref{it:bdry-sphere} has been established by \citet[Theorem~3.1(ii)]{richtarik2026local}.
    We include the proof as we re-use one of the intermediate results below.

    We already know that $x_{\rm lmo} \in \mathcal{B}(x,t)$, and therefore
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:norm_upper_corrected}
        {\left\| x_{\rm lmo}-x \right\|} \le t.
    \end{equation}
    It remains to prove the reverse inequality.
    We distinguish two cases.

  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@

  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{$\bullet$ Case 1: $\lambda>0$.}
    By the construction of \textup{(\ref{eq:kkt_corrected})}, $\lambda (x_{\rm lmo}-x) \in N_{\mathcal{B}(x,t)}(x_{\rm lmo})$.
    Since $x_{\rm lmo} \neq x$ and $\lambda > 0$, characterization \textup{(\ref{eq:normal_cone_ball_xx})} of the normal cone $N_{\mathcal{B}(x,t)}(x_{\rm lmo})$ implies that
    \begin{equation*}
        {\left\| x_{\rm lmo}-x \right\|} = t.
    \end{equation*}

     
  \@startsection{paragraph}{4}{\z@} 
                {1.5ex \@plus 0.5ex \@minus 0.2ex} 
                {-1em} 
                { 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize

  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{$\bullet$ Case 2: $\lambda=0$.}
    In this case, \textup{(\ref{eq:kkt_corrected})} becomes $\nabla f(x) + n = 0,$ i.e.,
    \begin{equation*}
        -\nabla f(x) \in N_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{\rm lmo}).
    \end{equation*}
    By the definition of the normal cone, this implies $\left\langle-\nabla f(x), z-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle \le 0$ for all $z \in \mathcal{X}$. 
    Equivalently,
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_{\rm lmo}-z\right\rangle \leq 0, \qquad \forall z \in \mathcal{X}.
    \end{equation*}
    In particular, for any $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{X}$,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:normalcone_xstar_equiv}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_{\rm lmo}-x_\star\right\rangle \le 0.
    \end{equation}

    Using the decomposition
    \begin{equation*}
        x_{\rm lmo}-x_\star = (x-x_\star)+(x_{\rm lmo}-x),
    \end{equation*}
    we obtain
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:inner_decomp}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_{\rm lmo}-x_\star\right\rangle = \left\langle\nabla f(x), x-x_\star\right\rangle + \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_{\rm lmo}-x\right\rangle.
    \end{equation}
    By the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:key_three_cs}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_{\rm lmo}-x\right\rangle \ge -{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|} {\left\| x_{\rm lmo}-x \right\|} \overset{\textup{(\ref{eq:norm_upper_corrected})}}{\ge} -{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|} t.
    \end{equation}
    By combining this with \textup{(\ref{eq:inner_decomp})}, we get
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_{\rm lmo}-x_\star\right\rangle \ge \left\langle\nabla f(x), x-x_\star\right\rangle-t{\left\|  \nabla f(x) \right\|}.
    \end{equation*}
    Using \textup{(\ref{eq:combined_admissibility_corrected})}, we conclude that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:halfspace_lower_corrected}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_{\rm lmo}-x_\star\right\rangle \ge 0.
    \end{equation}
    Combining \textup{(\ref{eq:normalcone_xstar_equiv})} and \textup{(\ref{eq:halfspace_lower_corrected})}, we get
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_{\rm lmo}-x_\star\right\rangle = 0.
    \end{equation*}
    Hence equality must hold in the preceding chain of inequalities, and in particular in \textup{(\ref{eq:key_three_cs})}.
    This implies
    \begin{equation*}
        {\left\| x_{\rm lmo}-x \right\|} = t ,
    \end{equation*}
    which proves \textup{(\ref{eq:lmo_step_has_length_t_combined})}.

\bf} 
{Step 6: Projection characterization.}
    We now prove that
    \begin{equation*}
        x_{\rm lmo} = {\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t))}(x).
    \end{equation*}
    Let $\mathcal{C} \subseteq \mathbb{R}^d$ be a non-empty closed convex set and let $x \in \mathbb{R}^d$. A point $u \in \mathcal{C}$ satisfies
    \begin{equation*}
        u = {\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{C}}(x)
    \end{equation*}
    if and only if
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:proj_characterization_corrected}
        \left\langlex-u, y-u\right\rangle \le 0, \qquad \forall y \in \mathcal{C}.
    \end{equation}
    Therefore, it suffices to prove that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:goal_projection_corrected}
        \left\langlex-x_{\rm lmo}, y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle \le 0, \qquad \forall y \in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t)).
    \end{equation}

    We again distinguish the same two cases as above.

\@xpt\@xipt
  \abovedisplayskip      7\p@ \@plus 2\p@ \@minus 5\p@

  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 4\p@ \@plus 3\p@ \@minus 3\p@
\bf} 
{$\bullet$ Case 1: $\lambda>0$.}
    Fix any $y \in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t))$.
    Since $n \in N_{\mathcal{X}}(x_{\rm lmo})$ and $y \in \mathcal{X}$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langlen, y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle \le 0.
    \end{equation*}
    Also, since $y \in \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t))$ and $\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t) = \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle \le 0.
    \end{equation*}
    Taking the inner product of \textup{(\ref{eq:kkt_corrected})} with $y-x_{\rm lmo}$, we obtain
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle + \left\langlen, y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle + \lambda\left\langlex_{\rm lmo}-x, y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle = 0.
    \end{equation*}
    The first two terms are non-positive, and hence $\lambda\left\langlex_{\rm lmo}-x, y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle \ge 0$. 
    Since $\lambda > 0$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langlex-x_{\rm lmo}, y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle \le 0.
    \end{equation*}
    Thus \textup{(\ref{eq:goal_projection_corrected})} holds in this case.

\bf} 
{$\bullet$ Case 2: $\lambda=0$.}
    As proved above, equality must hold in the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality \textup{(\ref{eq:key_three_cs})}, and therefore
    \begin{equation*}
        x-x_{\rm lmo} = t\,\frac{\nabla f(x)}{{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}}.
    \end{equation*}
    Now fix any $y \in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t))$.
    Since $y \in \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t))$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle \le 0.
    \end{equation*}
    Therefore
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langlex-x_{\rm lmo}, y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle = \frac{t}{{\left\| \nabla f(x) \right\|}}\left\langle\nabla f(x), y-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle \le 0.
    \end{equation*}
    Thus \textup{(\ref{eq:goal_projection_corrected})} holds in this case as well.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Write $g \coloneqq \nabla f(x)$, which is non-zero by \Cref{lem:zero_grad}.
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item 
        \Cref{thm:alpha_star_exact,lem:alpha_t_vs_alpha_cvx,lem:alpha_t_le_alpha_lmo} show that $\alpha_\star(x) \le \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) \le \alpha_{\rm proj}(x,t) \le \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t)$, which by \textup{(\ref{eq:level_depth_table})} is the chain $s_{\rm lmo}(x,t) \le t \le t(x) \le s_\star(x)$.
        For the remaining strict inequality $s_{\rm lmo}(x,t) > 0$, fix any $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$ and set $\mu \coloneqq t/{\left\| x-x_\star \right\|}$, which lies in $(0,1]$ because $0 < t \le t(x) \le \left\langleg, x-x_\star\right\rangle/{\left\| g \right\|} \le {\left\| x-x_\star \right\|}$ by \textup{(\ref{eq:combined_admissibility_corrected})} and Cauchy--Schwarz.
        The point $z \coloneqq (1-\mu)x+\mu x_\star$ lies in $\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{B}(x,t)$, so $\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t) \le \left\langleg, z\right\rangle = \left\langleg, x\right\rangle-\mu\left\langleg, x-x_\star\right\rangle$ and therefore $s_{\rm lmo}(x,t) = \left(\left\langleg, x\right\rangle-\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t)\right)/{\left\| g \right\|} \ge \mu\left\langleg, x-x_\star\right\rangle/{\left\| g \right\|} \ge \mu\left(f(x)-f_\star\right)/{\left\| g \right\|} > 0$; equivalently, $x \notin \mathcal{H}_x(\nabla f(x),s_{\rm lmo}(x,t))$.
        The same computation is recorded, in the notation of the local Frank--Wolfe gap, as \Cref{lem:local_fw_gap}.
        Since $\mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x), \alpha_\star(x))$ (\Cref{thm:alpha_star_exact}) and the half-spaces are increasing in the level $\alpha$, we have
        \begin{equation*}
            \mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x), \alpha_\star(x)) \subseteq \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x), \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t)) .
        \end{equation*}

        \item 
        This is the content of \textup{(\ref{eq:lmo_equals_projection_halfspace_combined})}, part~\ref{it:bdry-proj} of \Cref{thm:lmo_boundary_and_projection}.

        \item 
        This follows from \textup{(\ref{eq:lmo_step_has_length_t_combined})}, part~\ref{it:bdry-sphere} of \Cref{thm:lmo_boundary_and_projection}.
        Further, by part \ref{it:bdry-proj} of the same theorem, $x_{\rm lmo} = {\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{H}_{\rm lmo}}(x)$, so $\operatorname{dist}\left(x,\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{H}_{\rm lmo}\right) = {\left\| x-x_{\rm lmo} \right\|} = t$.

        \item 
        \Cref{thm:projection_fejer_halfspace} applied with $\alpha = \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x,t) \ge \alpha_\star(x)$, which holds by part~\ref{it:lmoproj-localizes}, combined with the step length in part~\ref{it:lmoproj-stepsize}.

        Since the minimizer is identified with a projection, it is unique: 
        by part~\ref{it:lmoproj-stepsize}, every element of $\mathop{\arg\!\min}_{z\in\mathcal{X} \cap\mathcal{B}(x,t)}\left\langleg, z\right\rangle$ lies on the sphere ${\left\| z-x \right\|} = t$, and that argmin is convex, so it is a singleton.
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:descent} 
    Let $\{x_k\}$ be generated by {\color{gray}

\sfLocal LMO}\xspace with admissible radii $0 < t_k \le t(x_k)$ from $x_0 \in \mathcal{X}$.
    Then, for every $k \ge 0$ and every $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$,
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item \label{it:descent-1}
        $t_k \le {\left\| x_k-x_\star \right\|}$,

        \item \label{it:descent-2}
        ${\left\| x_{k+1}-x_k \right\|} = t_k$,

        \item \label{it:descent-3}
        ${\left\| x_{k+1}-x_\star \right\|}^2 \le {\left\| x_k-x_\star \right\|}^2-t_k^2$.
    \end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
Let $g_k = \nabla f(x_k)$.
    Convexity and Cauchy--Schwarz give $f(x_k) - f_\star \le \left\langleg_k, x_k-x_\star\right\rangle \le {\left\| g_k \right\|}{\left\| x_k-x_\star \right\|}$, so $t_k \le t(x_k) = (f(x_k) - f_\star) / {\left\| g_k \right\|} \le {\left\| x_k-x_\star \right\|}$, which is part~\ref{it:descent-1}.
    Parts \ref{it:descent-2} and \ref{it:descent-3} correspond to parts~\ref{it:lmoproj-stepsize} and~\ref{it:lmoproj-distance} of \Cref{thm:lmo_is_projection}, respectively.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- head-2: body_md -->
\Cref{thm:universal_band} states that every projection method whose cut lies in the deep-cut band attains the rate $K^{-(1+\vartheta)/2}$, without knowledge of $\vartheta$ or $L_\vartheta$.
Our argument is based on two observations.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item
    \Cref{lem:polyak_cut} shows that a point $x_+$ in the Polyak half-space $\mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x),\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x))$ satisfies $f(x_+)-f_\star \le D_f(x_+,x)$.
    Cutting at or below the Polyak level converts the Bregman divergence into a function gap.

    \item
    Fejér monotonicity (\Cref{thm:projection_fejer_halfspace}) makes the squared step lengths summable, so the shortest of $K$ steps is at most $\operatorname{dist}(x_0,\mathcal{X}_\star)/\sqrt{K}$.
\end{enumerate}

Hölder continuity turns the first into $f(x_{k+1})-f_\star \le \frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}{\left\| x_{k+1}-x_k \right\|}^{1+\vartheta}$, and the second then gives the rate.
We stress that neither step uses $\vartheta$ or $L_\vartheta$, which is why the bound is universal.

\Cref{thm:alpha_star_exact,thm:projection_fejer_halfspace} of \Cref{app:identification} are re-used below.
<!-- end head-2 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:polyak_cut}
    Fix $x \in \mathcal{X}$ and let $x_+ \in \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x), \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x))$.
    Then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:special_Polyak_ineq}
        f(x_+) - f_\star \le D_f(x_+,x) .
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
By the definition \textup{(\ref{eq:alpha_cvx_def})} of $\alpha_{\rm cvx}$ and the assumption $x_+ \in \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x), \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x))$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_+\right\rangle \le \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x) = \left\langle\nabla f(x), x\right\rangle - f(x) + f_\star .
    \end{equation*}
    Hence, the Bregman divergence $D_f(x_+,x)$ can be bounded below as
    \begin{align*}
        D_f(x_+,x)
        & = f(x_+) - f(x) - \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_+-x\right\rangle\\
        & = f(x_+) - f_\star + \left(f_\star - f(x) + \left\langle\nabla f(x), x\right\rangle - \left\langle\nabla f(x), x_+\right\rangle\right) \\
        & \ge f(x_+) - f_\star .
    \end{align*}
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:universal_engine}
    Suppose $\nabla f$ is Hölder continuous with exponent $\vartheta \in [0,1]$ and constant $L_\vartheta$, in the sense that
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:band_holder}
        D_f(u,v) \le \frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}{\left\| u-v \right\|}^{1+\vartheta}, \qquad \forall u,v \in \mathbb{R}^d .
    \end{equation}
    Fix $x_0 \in \mathcal{X}$ and let $\{x_k\}_{k \ge 0} \subset \mathcal{X}$ be generated by any method with the following two properties:
    \begin{enumerate}
        \item
        \label{item:band_cut}
        $x_{k+1} \in \mathcal{H}(\nabla f(x_k), \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x_k))$ for all $k \ge 0$;

        \item
        \label{item:band_fejer}
        ${\left\| x_{k+1} - x_\star \right\|}^2 \le {\left\| x_k - x_\star \right\|}^2 - {\left\| x_{k+1} - x_k \right\|}^2$ for all $k \ge 0$ and all $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$.
    \end{enumerate}
    Then, for every $K \ge 1$,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:uy08fydo87fdo8f}
        \min_{0 \le k \le K-1} \left(f(x_{k+1})-f_\star\right) \le \frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta} \left(\frac{\operatorname{dist}^2(x_0,\mathcal{X}_\star)}{K}\right)^{\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}} .
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
By property \ref{item:band_cut}, \Cref{lem:polyak_cut} applies at $x = x_k$ with $x_+ = x_{k+1}$.
    Combining \textup{(\ref{eq:special_Polyak_ineq})} with the Hölder bound \textup{(\ref{eq:band_holder})}, used with $u = x_{k+1}$ and $v = x_k$, gives
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:0980d9gfg_89gyfg}
        f(x_{k+1}) - f_\star \le D_f(x_{k+1},x_k) \le \frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta} {\left\| x_{k+1}-x_k \right\|}^{1+\vartheta} .
    \end{equation}
    Property \ref{item:band_fejer} telescopes as in the proof of \Cref{thm:projection_fejer_halfspace} and yields
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:1/K_ineq}
        \min_{0 \le k \le K-1} {\left\| x_{k} - x_{k+1} \right\|}^2 \le \frac{\operatorname{dist}^2(x_0,\mathcal{X}_\star)}{K}, \qquad \forall K \ge 1 .
    \end{equation}
    Let $k^\star \in \mathop{\arg\!\min}_{0 \le k \le K-1}{\left\| x_{k+1}-x_k \right\|}$. 
    Applying \textup{(\ref{eq:0980d9gfg_89gyfg})} at $k = k^\star$ and then \textup{(\ref{eq:1/K_ineq})},
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{aligned}
            \min_{0 \le k \le K-1}\left(f(x_{k+1})-f_\star\right) \ &\le \ f(x_{k^\star+1})-f_\star \ \le \ \frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}{\left\| x_{k^\star+1}-x_{k^\star} \right\|}^{1+\vartheta}\\
            &\le \ \frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}\left(\frac{\operatorname{dist}^2(x_0,\mathcal{X}_\star)}{K}\right)^{\frac{1+\vartheta}{2}} ,
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation*}
    which is \textup{(\ref{eq:uy08fydo87fdo8f})}.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Fix $K \ge 1$. 
    If $x_k \in \mathcal{X}_\star$ for some $0 \le k \le K$, then $f(x_k) = f_\star$ and \textup{(\ref{eq:band_rate})} holds trivially, so we may assume $x_k \in \mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}_\star$ for every $0 \le k \le K$. 

    We write $\alpha_k \coloneqq \left\langleg_k, x_k\right\rangle-s_k{\left\| g_k \right\|}$ for the level of the $k$-th cut, so that $\mathcal{H}_{x_k}(g_k,s_k) = \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_k)$ by \textup{(\ref{eq:dictionary})} and the hypothesis $t(x_k) \le s_k \le s_\star(x_k)$ reads $\alpha_\star(x_k) \le \alpha_k \le \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x_k)$ by \textup{(\ref{eq:level_depth_table})}.
    We verify the two hypotheses of \Cref{lem:universal_engine}.
    The lower bound $\alpha_k \ge \alpha_\star(x_k)$ gives $\mathcal{X}_\star \subseteq \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_k)$ by \Cref{thm:alpha_star_exact}, so \Cref{thm:projection_fejer_halfspace} applies and the iterates satisfy property \ref{item:band_fejer}.
    The upper bound $\alpha_k \le \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x_k)$ gives $\mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_k) \subseteq \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x_k))$, and since $x_{k+1} \in \mathcal{X} \cap \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_k)$ by construction, property \ref{item:band_cut} holds as well.
    \Cref{lem:universal_engine} then gives \textup{(\ref{eq:uy08fydo87fdo8f})}.
    Its minimum is taken over the $K$ iterates $x_1,\dots,x_K$ produced by the method, so it is sharper than \textup{(\ref{eq:band_rate})}, whose minimum ranges over $x_0,\dots,x_K$ as well; 
    we state the weaker form in the main text for consistency among \textup{(\ref{eq:band_rate})}, \textup{(\ref{eq:localrate})} and \textup{(\ref{eq:rate_zero})}.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- head-3: body_md -->
{\color{gray}

  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\sfLocal LMO}\xspace cuts above the Polyak level $\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x)$, so the analysis of \Cref{app:band} does not apply.
For convenience, we define the suboptimality gap of iterate $x_k$ as
\begin{equation}
    a_k \coloneqq f(x_k) - f_\star .
\end{equation}

We further define the projection of the initial iterate onto the set of minimizers
\begin{equation}
    \label{eq:xstar0}
    x_\star^0 \coloneqq {\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_\star}(x_0) ,
\end{equation}
and write $r_k \coloneqq {\left\| x_k-x_\star^0 \right\|}$, so that $r_0 = R \coloneqq \operatorname{dist}(x_0,\mathcal{X}_\star)$.
We also write $G_\star \coloneqq {\left\| \nabla f(x_\star) \right\|}$, which by \Cref{lem:grad_const_on_Xstar} does not depend on the choice of $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$.

Our proof proceeds in four steps.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item
    \label{item:ingr_holder}
    In \Cref{sub:holder}, we establish the conjugate Hölder inequality to give the gradient bound ${\left\| g_k-\nabla f(x_\star) \right\|} \le \kappa a_k^{\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)}$ of \Cref{lem:grad_bound}---whence, by the triangle inequality, the two-sided bound $\bigl|{\left\| g_k \right\|}-G_\star\bigr| \le \kappa a_k^{\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)}$ used in \textup{(\ref{eq:twosided})}---and, with Fejér monotonicity, a priori bounds that require no compactness.

    \item
    \Cref{sub:gap} tackles the local Frank--Wolfe gap and the overshoot.
    With $\ell_k \coloneqq \left\langleg_k, x_k-x_{k+1}\right\rangle$ and $\rho_k \coloneqq \ell_k/a_k \in (0,1]$, the descent inequality reads $a_{k+1} \le a_k(1-\rho_k+\delta_k)$ with $\delta_k = c\,t_k^{1+\vartheta}/a_k$, and the overshoot $\Gamma_k = (1-\rho_k) a_k$ provides a floor on the next function value.

    \item
    In \Cref{sub:budget}, we use the projection identity of \Cref{app:identification} to tighten $r_{k+1}^2 \le r_k^2-t_k^2$ to $r_{k+1}^2 \le r_k^2-t_k^2(2-\rho_k)/\rho_k$, whence $\sum_{k<K}t_k^2/\rho_k \le R^2$.
    Here, $\rho_k \in (0,1]$ is the fraction of the available first-order decrease captured by the linear minimization oracle; 
    at the Polyak radius $t_k = t(x_k)$ it is the cosine of the angle between the gradient $\nabla f(x_k)$ and the step $x_k - x_{k+1}$.
    The function value can fall at most a $\rho_k$ fraction (\Cref{lem:overshoot_lower_bound}), while the squared distance falls by $t_k^2 / \rho_k$.

    \item
    \Cref{sub:proof6} assembles the proof of \Cref{thm:localrate}.
    Let $A = \min_{k\le K} a_k$.
    If $A$ is above the level $\bar a$ at which the Hölder part of the gradient dominates $G_\star$, the Fejér budget alone gives the exponent $\tfrac{1+\vartheta}2$.
    Below $\bar a$ the two-sided bound of step \ref{item:ingr_holder} pins ${\left\| g_k \right\|}$ to within a constant factor of $G_\star$, and the $K$ steps are counted three ways \citep{cartis2011adaptive,cartis2012evaluation}: 
    those above $\bar a$ (few, by the budget); 
    those that stall, $\rho_k < 2\delta_k$ (few, because each spends $a_kt_k^{1-\vartheta}/2c$ of the budget); 
    and the productive rest, counted by Cauchy--Schwarz against the potential $\Phi_k = a_k^{-2}$, using the identity \textup{(\ref{eq:gaincost})}.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end head-3 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:conj_holder}
    Let $f:\mathbb{R}^d \to \mathbb{R}$ be convex and differentiable with $D_f(u,v) \le \frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}{\left\| u-v \right\|}^{1+\vartheta}$ for all $u,v$, where $\vartheta \in (0,1]$.
    Then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:conj_holder}
        D_f(u,v)\  \ge \ \frac{\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}\,L_\vartheta^{-1/\vartheta}\, {\left\| \nabla f(u)-\nabla f(v) \right\|}^{\frac{1+\vartheta}{\vartheta}}\qquad\forall u,v \in \mathbb{R}^d .
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Fix $v$ and set $\varphi(w) \coloneqq D_f(w,v) = f(w)-f(v)-\left\langle\nabla f(v), w-v\right\rangle$.
    Then $\varphi$ is convex and differentiable with $\nabla\varphi(w) = \nabla f(w)-\nabla f(v)$ and $\varphi \ge 0$ by convexity of $f$.
    Since $\varphi = f-\ell$ with $\ell(w) \coloneqq f(v) + \left\langle\nabla f(v), w-v\right\rangle$ affine, and $D_\ell \equiv 0$ for affine $\ell$ because $\nabla\ell$ is constant, we have $D_\varphi = D_f-D_\ell = D_f$.
    Therefore, $\varphi$ obeys the same Hölder bound, with the same $L_\vartheta$.
    
    Fix $u$ and put $\Delta \coloneqq {\left\| \nabla\varphi(u) \right\|}$.
    If $\Delta = 0$, there is nothing to prove.
    
    Otherwise, let, for $s > 0$, $w \coloneqq u - s \nabla\varphi(u) / \Delta$.
    Then
    \begin{equation*}
        0 \le \varphi(w) = \varphi(u) + \left\langle\nabla\varphi(u), w-u\right\rangle+D_\varphi(w,u) \le \varphi(u) - s \Delta + \frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta} s^{1+\vartheta}.
    \end{equation*}
    Choosing $s = (\Delta/L_\vartheta)^{1/\vartheta}$ makes $\frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}s^{1+\vartheta} = \frac{L_\vartheta s^\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}\,s = \frac{s\Delta}{1+\vartheta}$, so $0 \le \varphi(u)-\frac{\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}s\Delta$, which is \textup{(\ref{eq:conj_holder})}.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:grad_bound_zero}
    Let $f$ satisfy $D_f(u,v) \le L_0{\left\| u-v \right\|}$ for all $u,v \in \mathbb{R}^d$.
    Then
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:grad_bound_zero}
        {\left\| \nabla f(u)-\nabla f(v) \right\|}\  \le \ L_0, \qquad \forall u,v \in \mathbb{R}^d .
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Fix $v$ and set $\varphi(w) \coloneqq D_f(w,v)$, so that $\varphi$ is convex and differentiable with $\nabla\varphi(w) = \nabla f(w)-\nabla f(v)$, $\varphi \ge 0$, and $D_\varphi = D_f$, exactly as in the proof of \Cref{lem:conj_holder}.
    Fix $u$ and put $\Delta \coloneqq {\left\| \nabla\varphi(u) \right\|}$; if $\Delta = 0$ there is nothing to prove.
    For $s > 0$ let $w_s \coloneqq u-s\nabla\varphi(u)/\Delta$, so that ${\left\| w_s-u \right\|} = s$ and
    \begin{equation*}
        0\  \le \ \varphi(w_s)\  = \ \varphi(u)+\left\langle\nabla\varphi(u), w_s-u\right\rangle+D_\varphi(w_s,u)\  \le \ \varphi(u)-s\Delta+L_0\,s .
    \end{equation*}
    Hence $s\left(\Delta-L_0\right) \le \varphi(u)$ for every $s > 0$.
    Were $\Delta > L_0$, the left-hand side would be unbounded above, a contradiction; so $\Delta \le L_0$.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:grad_bound}
    Let $\nabla f$ be Hölder continuous with exponent $\vartheta \in [0,1]$ and constant $L_\vartheta$.
    Then, for every $x \in \mathcal{X}$ and every $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:grad_bound}
        {\left\| \nabla f(x)-\nabla f(x_\star) \right\|}\  \le \ \kappa_\vartheta\,L_\vartheta^{\frac{1}{1+\vartheta}}\, \left(f(x)-f_\star\right)^{\frac{\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}} .
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
First-order optimality of $x_\star$ over $\mathcal{X}$ gives $\left\langle\nabla f(x_\star), x-x_\star\right\rangle \ge 0$ for all $x \in \mathcal{X}$, whence $D_f(x,x_\star) = f(x)-f_\star-\left\langle\nabla f(x_\star), x-x_\star\right\rangle \le f(x)-f_\star$.
    Combining with \Cref{lem:conj_holder} applied at $(u,v) = (x,x_\star)$, we find
    \begin{equation*}
        \frac{\vartheta}{1+\vartheta} L_\vartheta^{-1/\vartheta} {\left\| \nabla f(x)-\nabla f(x_\star) \right\|}^{(1+\vartheta)/\vartheta} \le f(x) - f_\star .
    \end{equation*}
    Raising to the power $\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)$ and rearranging gives \textup{(\ref{eq:grad_bound})}.
    For $\vartheta = 0$, \textup{(\ref{eq:grad_bound})} reads ${\left\| \nabla f(x)-\nabla f(x_\star) \right\|} \le \kappa_0L_0 = L_0$, which is \Cref{lem:grad_bound_zero}.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:apriori}
    Along any {\color{gray}

  \@setfontsize\small\@ixpt\@xpt

\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\sfLocal LMO}\xspace run with $0 < t_k \le t(x_k)$ starting from $x_0 \in \mathcal{X}$, we have
    \begin{align}
    \label{eq:apriori}
        a_k & \le a_{\max} \coloneqq \max\left\{2G_\star R,\ (2\kappa_\vartheta)^{1+\vartheta}L_\vartheta R^{1+\vartheta}\right\} \\
        {\left\| g_k \right\|} & \le G \coloneqq G_\star + \kappa_\vartheta L_\vartheta^{\frac1{1+\vartheta}} a_{\max}^{\frac{\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}} < +\infty ,
    \end{align}
    and consequently
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:polyak_radius_lb}
        t(x_k) = \frac{a_k}{{\left\| g_k \right\|}} \ge \frac{a_k}{G_\star+\kappa\,a_k^{\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)}} = h(a_k),
    \end{equation}
    with $h$ as in \textup{(\ref{eq:kappa_h})}, and $h$ is strictly increasing on $[0,+\infty)$.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Convexity and the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality give
    \begin{equation*}
        a_k \le \left\langleg_k, x_k-x_\star^0\right\rangle \le {\left\| g_k \right\|}r_k \le {\left\| g_k \right\|} R ,
    \end{equation*}
    where the last step follows from Fejér monotonicity (part~\ref{it:descent-3} of \Cref{lem:descent}).
    With \textup{(\ref{eq:grad_bound})}, 
    \begin{equation}
        a_k \le G_\star R + \kappa_\vartheta L_\vartheta^{1/(1+\vartheta)}R \, a_k^{\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)} .
    \end{equation}
    
    Now, if $a \le u+va^{p}$ with $p = \frac{\vartheta}{1+\vartheta} \in [0,\tfrac{1}{2}]$ and $u,v \ge 0$, then either $a \le 2u$ or $a \le 2va^{p}$, i.e. $a \le (2v)^{1/(1-p)} = (2v)^{1+\vartheta}$.
    With $u = G_\star R$ and $v = \kappa R$ this is the first bound in \textup{(\ref{eq:apriori})}, and the second follows from \textup{(\ref{eq:grad_bound})} again.

    Equation \textup{(\ref{eq:polyak_radius_lb})} is just \textup{(\ref{eq:grad_bound})} together with ${\left\| g_k \right\|} \le G_\star+{\left\| g_k-\nabla f(x_\star) \right\|}$ for any $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$.

    Finally $h(a) = a/(G_\star+\kappa a^{p})$ with $p = \tfrac{\vartheta}{1+\vartheta} \in [0,\tfrac12]$ has $h'(a) = \left(G_\star+(1-p)\kappa a^{p}\right)/(G_\star+\kappa a^{p})^2 > 0$ for $a > 0$;
    if $G_\star = 0$ this reads $h(a) = a^{1/(1+\vartheta)}/\kappa$, which is continuous at $0$ with our convention that $h(0) = 0$, so $h$ is increasing on all of $[0,\infty)$ in either case.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def:local_fw_gap}
    For $x \in \mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}_\star$ and $t > 0$, define
    \begin{equation*}
        \ell(x,t) \coloneqq \left\langle\nabla f(x), x\right\rangle-\min_{z\in\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{B}(x,t)}\left\langle\nabla f(x), z\right\rangle = \left\langle\nabla f(x), x-x_{\rm lmo}\right\rangle\  \ge 0 .
    \end{equation*}
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:local_fw_gap}
    Fix $x \in \mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}_\star$ and $0 < t \le t(x)$.
    Then for every $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:local_fw_gap}
        \ell(x,t)\  \ge \ \frac{t}{{\left\| x-x_\star \right\|}}\,\left\langle\nabla f(x), x-x_\star\right\rangle\  \ge \ \frac{t}{{\left\| x-x_\star \right\|}}\,\left(f(x)-f_\star\right).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Let $g = \nabla f(x)$.
    As in steps~2 and~3 of the proof of \Cref{thm:lmo_boundary_and_projection}, $0 < t \le t(x) \le \left\langleg, x-x_\star\right\rangle/{\left\| g \right\|} \le {\left\| x-x_\star \right\|}$, so $\lambda \coloneqq t/{\left\| x-x_\star \right\|} \in (0,1]$ and $z_t \coloneqq x-\lambda(x-x_\star) = (1-\lambda)x+\lambda x_\star \in \mathcal{X}$ with ${\left\| z_t-x \right\|} = t$; hence $z_t \in \mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{B}(x,t)$.
    Therefore
    \begin{equation*}
        \min_{z\in\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{B}(x,t)}\left\langleg, z\right\rangle \le \left\langleg, z_t\right\rangle = \left\langleg, x\right\rangle-\lambda\left\langleg, x-x_\star\right\rangle,
    \end{equation*}
    so $\ell(x,t) \ge \lambda\left\langleg, x-x_\star\right\rangle$.
    The last inequality in \textup{(\ref{eq:local_fw_gap})} is first-order convexity, $\left\langleg, x-x_\star\right\rangle \ge f(x)-f_\star$.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem:overshoot}
    Fix $x_k \in \mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}_\star$ and $0 < t_k \le t(x_k)$, and let $x_{k+1}$ be a {\color{gray}

\sfLocal LMO}\xspace step.
    With $\ell_k \coloneqq \ell(x_k,t_k)$,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:overshoot}
        \left\langleg_k, x_{k+1}\right\rangle = \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x_k,t_k) = \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x_k)+\Gamma_k, \qquad \Gamma_k \coloneqq a_k-\ell_k = \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x_k,t_k)-\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x_k),
    \end{equation}
    and $\Gamma_k$ satisfies
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:overshoot_bounds}
        0\  \le \ \alpha_{\rm proj}(x_k,t_k)-\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x_k)\  \le \ \Gamma_k\  \le \ a_k\left(1-\tfrac{t_k}{{\left\| x_k-x_\star \right\|}}\right) , \qquad \forall x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star.
    \end{equation}
    Consequently $x_{k+1} \in \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_{\rm proj}(x_k,t_k))$ if and only if $\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x_k,t_k) = \alpha_{\rm proj}(x_k,t_k)$, i.e. iff the unconstrained ball minimizer $x_k-t_k\,g_k/{\left\| g_k \right\|}$ is feasible in $\mathcal{X}$.
    Whenever the constraint $\mathcal{X}$ is active at the step, $x_{k+1} \notin \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_{\rm proj}(x_k,t_k))$.
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
By \Cref{thm:lmo_boundary_and_projection}, $\left\langleg_k, x_{k+1}\right\rangle = \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x_k,t_k)$.
    By definition $\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x_k) = \left\langleg_k, x_k\right\rangle-a_k$, so $\alpha_{\rm lmo}(x_k,t_k)-\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x_k) = \left\langleg_k, x_{k+1}\right\rangle-\left\langleg_k, x_k\right\rangle+a_k = a_k-\ell_k = \Gamma_k$, which is \textup{(\ref{eq:overshoot})}.
    For \textup{(\ref{eq:overshoot_bounds})}: the middle inequality $\Gamma_k \ge \alpha_{\rm proj}-\alpha_{\rm cvx} \ge 0$ is \Cref{lem:alpha_t_le_alpha_lmo} together with \Cref{lem:alpha_t_vs_alpha_cvx}; the Cauchy--Schwarz bound $\ell_k = \left\langleg_k, x_k-x_{k+1}\right\rangle \le {\left\| g_k \right\|}\,{\left\| x_k-x_{k+1} \right\|} = t_k{\left\| g_k \right\|} \le t(x_k){\left\| g_k \right\|} = a_k$ gives $\Gamma_k \ge 0$; and the upper bound is \Cref{lem:local_fw_gap}.
    The final claim is \textup{(\ref{eq:overshoot})}: $x_{k+1} \in \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha_{\rm proj}) \Leftrightarrow \alpha_{\rm lmo} \le \alpha_{\rm proj} \Leftrightarrow \alpha_{\rm lmo} = \alpha_{\rm proj}$, using $\alpha_{\rm lmo} \ge \alpha_{\rm proj}$ (\Cref{lem:alpha_t_le_alpha_lmo}).
    Equality holds iff the minimum of $\left\langleg_k, \cdot\right\rangle$ over $\mathcal{B}(x_k,t_k)$, attained at $x_k-t_k g_k/{\left\| g_k \right\|}$, is attained inside $\mathcal{X}$.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
