\sfLocal LMO}\xspace step can push the suboptimality below the overshoot $\Gamma_k$, however smooth $f$ is.
    By \textup{(\ref{eq:overshoot_bounds})}, $\Gamma_k \ge \alpha_{\rm proj}(x_k,t_k)-\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x_k) = {\left\| g_k \right\|}\left(t(x_k)-t_k\right)$, so the floor vanishes only if $t_k = t(x_k)$ \emph{and} $\alpha_{\rm lmo} = \alpha_{\rm proj}$, i.e. only if the radius is the full Polyak radius and the unconstrained ball minimizer is feasible --- in which case the step coincides with the Polyak projection step.
    A shorter radius leaves a positive floor even when $\mathcal{X}$ is inactive.
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
By the definition of the Bregman divergence and of $\ell_k$,
    \begin{equation*}
        D_f(x_{k+1},x_k) = f(x_{k+1})-f(x_k)-\left\langleg_k, x_{k+1}-x_k\right\rangle = f(x_{k+1})-f(x_k)+\ell_k .
    \end{equation*}
    Adding and subtracting $f_\star$ and using $\Gamma_k = a_k-\ell_k$ \textup{(\ref{eq:overshoot})} gives $f(x_{k+1})-f_\star = D_f(x_{k+1},x_k)+a_k-\ell_k = D_f(x_{k+1},x_k)+\Gamma_k$.
    Since $D_f(x_{k+1},x_k) \ge 0$ by convexity and $\Gamma_k \ge 0$ by \textup{(\ref{eq:overshoot_bounds})}, we obtain \textup{(\ref{eq:overshoot_lower_bound})}.
<!-- end proof-24 -->

\sfLocal LMO}\xspace with $0 < t_k \le t(x_k)$.
    With $R = \operatorname{dist}(x_0,\mathcal{X}_\star)$, for every $k \ge 0$,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:master_recursion}
        a_{k+1}\  \le \ a_k-\ell_k+\frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}\,t_k^{\,1+\vartheta} \  \le \ \left(1-\tfrac{t_k}{R}\right)a_k+\frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}\,t_k^{\,1+\vartheta}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
The Hölder descent inequality with $u = x_{k+1}$, $v = x_k$ gives $a_{k+1} = a_k-\ell_k+D_f(x_{k+1},x_k) \le a_k-\ell_k+\frac{L_\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}{\left\| x_{k+1}-x_k \right\|}^{1+\vartheta}$, and ${\left\| x_{k+1}-x_k \right\|} = t_k$ by \Cref{thm:lmo_boundary_and_projection}; this is the first inequality.
    For the second, take $x_\star = {\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{X}_\star}(x_0)$, so that part~\ref{it:descent-3} of \Cref{lem:descent} gives ${\left\| x_k-x_\star \right\|} \le {\left\| x_0-x_\star \right\|} = R$, so \Cref{lem:local_fw_gap} yields $\ell_k \ge (t_k/{\left\| x_k-x_\star \right\|})a_k \ge (t_k/R)a_k$.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:sharp_fejer}
    Let $x_k \in \mathcal{X} \setminus \mathcal{X}_\star$, let $0 < t_k \le t(x_k)$, and let $x_{k+1}$ be a {\color{gray}

  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\sfLocal LMO}\xspace step.
    Put $\ell_k = \left\langleg_k, x_k-x_{k+1}\right\rangle\ ( > 0)$ and, for each $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \varepsilon_k(x_\star) \coloneqq \left\langleg_k, x_{k+1}-x_\star\right\rangle \ge \Gamma_k = a_k-\ell_k \ge 0 .
    \end{equation*}
    Then, for every $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:sharp_fejer}
        {\left\| x_{k+1}-x_\star \right\|}^2 \le {\left\| x_k-x_\star \right\|}^2 - t_k^2\left(1+\frac{2\varepsilon_k(x_\star)}{\ell_k}\right) \le {\left\| x_k-x_\star \right\|}^2 - t_k^2\left(1+\frac{2\Gamma_k}{\ell_k}\right).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
\Cref{lem:local_fw_gap} shows that $\ell_k \ge (t_k/r_k)a_k > 0$, with $r_k = {\left\| x_k-x_\star^0 \right\|}$ as in \textup{(\ref{eq:xstar0})}.
    
    To establish $\varepsilon_k(x_\star) \ge \Gamma_k \ge 0$, note that convexity gives $\left\langleg_k, x_k-x_\star\right\rangle \ge a_k$, i.e. $\left\langleg_k, x_\star\right\rangle \le \alpha_{\rm cvx}(x_k)$. 
    Hence, $\varepsilon_k(x_\star) = \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x_k,t_k)-\left\langleg_k, x_\star\right\rangle \ge \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x_k,t_k)-\alpha_{\rm cvx}(x_k) = \Gamma_k$ by \Cref{thm:lmo_boundary_and_projection} and \textup{(\ref{eq:overshoot})}, and $\Gamma_k \ge 0$ by \Cref{lem:overshoot}.

    Write $\alpha \coloneqq \alpha_{\rm lmo}(x_k,t_k)$ and $\mathcal{H}_k \coloneqq \mathcal{H}(g_k,\alpha)$, so that $\left\langleg_k, x_k\right\rangle = \alpha+\ell_k$ and $\left\langleg_k, x_\star\right\rangle = \alpha - \varepsilon_k(x_\star)$.
    Set
    \begin{equation*}
        s \coloneqq \frac{\ell_k}{\ell_k+\varepsilon_k(x_\star)} \in (0,1] , \qquad z \coloneqq (1-s) x_k + s x_\star .
    \end{equation*}
    Then $z \in \mathcal{X}$ by convexity, and
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\langleg_k, z\right\rangle = (1-s)(\alpha+\ell_k)+s(\alpha-\varepsilon_k(x_\star)) = \alpha+\ell_k-s(\ell_k+\varepsilon_k(x_\star)) = \alpha,
    \end{equation*}
    so $z \in \mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{H}_k$.

    By \Cref{thm:lmo_boundary_and_projection}, $x_{k+1} = {\rm Proj}_{\mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{H}_k}(x_k)$, so the projection inequality at the point $z \in \mathcal{X}\cap\mathcal{H}_k$ reads $\left\langlex_k-x_{k+1}, z-x_{k+1}\right\rangle \le 0$.
    Since $z-x_{k+1} = (x_k-x_{k+1})-s(x_k-x_\star)$ and ${\left\| x_k-x_{k+1} \right\|} = t_k$ (\Cref{thm:lmo_boundary_and_projection}), this is
    \begin{equation*}
        t_k^2 - s\,\left\langlex_k-x_{k+1}, x_k-x_\star\right\rangle \le 0, \qquad\text{i.e.}\qquad \left\langlex_k-x_{k+1}, x_k-x_\star\right\rangle \ge \frac{t_k^2}{s} = t_k^2\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon_k(x_\star)}{\ell_k}\right).
    \end{equation*}
    Finally, ${\left\| x_{k+1}-x_\star \right\|}^2 = {\left\| x_k-x_\star \right\|}^2-2\left\langlex_k-x_{k+1}, x_k-x_\star\right\rangle+t_k^2$, which gives \textup{(\ref{eq:sharp_fejer})}. 
    The second inequality is $\varepsilon_k(x_\star) \ge \Gamma_k$.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
\label{rem:overshoot_pays}
    At the Polyak radius $t_k = t(x_k)$, with $x_k \in \mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}_\star$, one has $\ell_k \le {\left\| g_k \right\|}t_k = a_k$, so, specializing \textup{(\ref{eq:sharp_fejer})} to $x_\star = x_\star^0$ and writing $\rho_k \coloneqq \ell_k/a_k \in (0,1]$, \textup{(\ref{eq:sharp_fejer})} reads
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:sharp_fejer_polyak}
        r_{k+1}^2 \le r_k^2-t_k^2\,\frac{2-\rho_k}{\rho_k} \le r_k^2-\frac{t_k^2}{\rho_k}.
    \end{equation}
    \Cref{lem:overshoot_lower_bound} now says that the step cannot reduce the value below $\Gamma_k = (1-\rho_k) a_k$: the smaller $\rho_k$, the worse the value progress.
    On the other hand, \textup{(\ref{eq:sharp_fejer_polyak})} says the smaller $\rho_k$, the better the distance progress, by precisely the reciprocal factor.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cor:budget}
    For {\color{gray} 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize

\sfLocal LMO}\xspace at the Polyak radius $t_k = t(x_k)$, with $x_k \in \mathcal{X}\setminus\mathcal{X}_\star$ for every $k < K$, for every $K \ge 1$,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:budget}
        \sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\frac{t_k^2}{\rho_k} = \sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\frac{t_k^2\,a_k}{\ell_k} \le R^2 .
    \end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
This follows by telescoping \textup{(\ref{eq:sharp_fejer_polyak})} and using $r_K^2 \ge 0$.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lem:constants}
    For every $\vartheta \in [0,1]$, $D_\vartheta \le \tfrac{25}2$ and $E_\vartheta \le 25$, both attained at $\vartheta = 1$.
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Write $\psi(\vartheta) \coloneqq \kappa_\vartheta^{1+\vartheta} = \left(\tfrac{1+\vartheta}{\vartheta}\right)^{\vartheta} = \exp\left(\vartheta\log(1+\tfrac1\vartheta)\right)$.
    Its exponent has derivative $\log(1+\tfrac1\vartheta)-\tfrac1{1+\vartheta} \ge 0$, because $\log(1+x) \ge \tfrac{x}{1+x}$ with $x = 1/\vartheta$ gives exactly $\log(1+\tfrac1\vartheta) \ge \tfrac{1/\vartheta}{1+1/\vartheta} = \tfrac1{1+\vartheta}$.
    So $\psi$ is nondecreasing with $\psi(\vartheta) \le \psi(1) = 2$.
    Hence $D_\vartheta = (\tfrac52)^{1+\vartheta}\psi(\vartheta) \le (\tfrac52)^2\cdot2 = \tfrac{25}2$, attained at $\vartheta = 1$.

    For $E_\vartheta$ note first that $\tfrac1{2-\vartheta} \le 1$, so a bracket that is $\ge 1$ is not increased by raising it to that power; this settles the first bracket, while the second needs the sharper bound below.
    The first bracket is $\tfrac{25}9\,2^{\vartheta}(\tfrac32)^{1+\vartheta}\psi(\vartheta) \le \tfrac{25}9\cdot2\cdot\tfrac94\cdot2 = 25$, with equality at $\vartheta = 1$, so $E^{(1)}_\vartheta \le 25$ and $E^{(1)}_1 = 25$.
    For the second, put $B(\vartheta) \coloneqq 8\left(1+5^{(1+\vartheta)/2}\right)(\tfrac53)^{1-\vartheta}/(1+\vartheta)$, so $E^{(2)}_\vartheta = B(\vartheta)^{1/(2-\vartheta)}$, and it suffices to show $B(\vartheta) \le 25^{\,2-\vartheta}$.
    Since $5^{(1+\vartheta)/2} \le 5$ on $[0,1]$ we have $B(\vartheta) \le \widetilde B(\vartheta) \coloneqq 48(\tfrac53)^{1-\vartheta}/(1+\vartheta)$, and
    \begin{equation*}
        \frac{d}{d\vartheta}\log\frac{25^{\,2-\vartheta}}{\widetilde B(\vartheta)} = \frac1{1+\vartheta}-\log25+\log\tfrac53\  \le \ 1-3.218+0.512\  < \ 0 ,
    \end{equation*}
    so $25^{2-\vartheta}/\widetilde B(\vartheta)$ is decreasing and attains its minimum on $[0,1]$ at $\vartheta = 1$, where it equals $25/48\cdot2 = \tfrac{50}{48} > 1$.
    Hence $E^{(2)}_\vartheta \le \widetilde B(\vartheta)^{1/(2-\vartheta)} \le 25$.
    Finally, $E^{(3)}_\vartheta = \tfrac{20}3 \le 25$.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{prop:rate_zero}
    Let $\vartheta = 0$, that is, $D_f(u,v) \le L_0{\left\| u-v \right\|}$ for all $u,v \in \mathbb{R}^d$, and let $\{x_k\}$ be generated by {\color{gray} 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize 
  \@setfontsize\small\@ixpt\@xpt

\@ixpt\@xpt

  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\@ixpt\@xpt
  \abovedisplayskip      6\p@ \@plus 1.5\p@ \@minus 4\p@
  \abovedisplayshortskip \z@  \@plus 2\p@
  \belowdisplayskip      \abovedisplayskip
  \belowdisplayshortskip 3\p@ \@plus 2\p@   \@minus 2\p@
\sfLocal LMO}\xspace at the Polyak radius $t_k = t(x_k)$ from $x_0 \in \mathcal{X}$.
    Then, for every $K \ge 1$,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:rate_zero}
        \min_{0\le k\le K} a_k\  \le \ \frac{\left(G_\star+L_0\right)R}{\sqrt K} .
    \end{equation}
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
By \Cref{lem:grad_bound_zero} applied at $(u,v) = (x_k,x_\star)$ for any $x_\star \in \mathcal{X}_\star$, ${\left\| g_k \right\|} \le {\left\| \nabla f(x_\star) \right\|}+{\left\| g_k-\nabla f(x_\star) \right\|} \le G_\star+L_0$.
    At the Polyak radius $a_k = t_k{\left\| g_k \right\|}$, so $a_k \le \left(G_\star+L_0\right)t_k$ for every $k$.
    By part~\ref{it:descent-3} of \Cref{lem:descent} and $r_0 = R$, telescoping gives $\sum_{k<K}t_k^2 \le r_0^2-r_K^2 \le R^2$, hence $\min_{k<K}t_k^2 \le R^2/K$.
    Choosing $k^\star \in \mathop{\arg\!\min}_{k<K}t_k$ (so that $k^\star \le K-1$, and the bound in fact holds for $\min_{0\le k\le K-1}a_k$),
    \begin{equation*}
        \min_{0\le k\le K} a_k\  \le \ a_{k^\star}\  \le \ \left(G_\star+L_0\right)t_{k^\star}\  \le \ \frac{\left(G_\star+L_0\right)R}{\sqrt K} . \qedhere
    \end{equation*}
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
Suppose first that $\vartheta = 0$, so that both exponents in \textup{(\ref{eq:localrate})} equal $\tfrac12$ and the claim reads $\min_{k\le K}a_k \le \max\left\{\Lambda_1,\Lambda_2\right\}K^{-1/2}$ with $\Lambda_1 = \tfrac{25}2L_0R$ and $\Lambda_2 \ge 25\,G_\star R$, by \textup{(\ref{eq:lambdas})}.
    Since $u+v \le \max\left\{\tfrac{25}2u,25v\right\}$ for all $u,v \ge 0$, \Cref{prop:rate_zero} yields the claim.
    Assume from now on that $\vartheta \in (0,1]$.

    Set $A \coloneqq \min_{0\le k\le K} a_k$.
    If $A = 0$ there is nothing to prove, so assume $A > 0$; then $a_k \ge A > 0$ for every $k \le K$, and every $t_k$, $\rho_k$, $\delta_k$ below is well defined and finite.
    We use throughout: $\rho_k \in (0,1]$ (\Cref{lem:local_fw_gap} and \Cref{lem:overshoot}); $t_k \ge h(a_k) \ge h(A)$ with $h$ increasing (\Cref{cor:apriori}); $\sum_{k<K}t_k^2 \le \sum_{k<K}t_k^2/\rho_k \le R^2$ (\Cref{cor:budget}); and the \emph{two-sided} gradient bound
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:twosided}
        G_\star-\kappa\,a_k^{\frac{\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}}\  \le \ {\left\| g_k \right\|}\  \le \ G_\star+\kappa\,a_k^{\frac{\vartheta}{1+\vartheta}},
    \end{equation}
    which is \textup{(\ref{eq:grad_bound})} together with the triangle inequality in both directions.

    \emph{Case 1: $A > \bar a$.} 
    We may assume $\kappa > 0$: if $L_\vartheta = 0$ then $\kappa = 0$, and either $G_\star > 0$, in which case $\bar a = +\infty$ by the convention following \textup{(\ref{eq:abar})} and this case is empty, or $G_\star = 0$, in which case $D_f \equiv 0$ forces $f$ to be affine with $\nabla f \equiv \nabla f(x_\star) = 0$, so $a_0 = 0$ and $A = 0$, already excluded.

    By \textup{(\ref{eq:abar})} this means $\kappa A^{\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)} > \lambda G_\star$, hence $G_\star+\kappa A^{\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)} < \tfrac{1+\lambda}{\lambda}\kappa A^{\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)}$ and
    \begin{equation*}
        h(A)\  = \ \frac{A}{G_\star+\kappa A^{\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)}}\  > \ \frac{\lambda}{(1+\lambda)\kappa}\,A^{\frac1{1+\vartheta}} .
    \end{equation*}
    Since $t_k \ge h(A)$ for all $k < K$, the Fejér budget gives $K\,h(A)^2 \le R^2$, i.e. $A^{2/(1+\vartheta)} \le \left(\tfrac{(1+\lambda)\kappa}{\lambda}\right)^2R^2/K$, i.e.
    \begin{equation*}
        A\  \le \ \left(\tfrac{1+\lambda}{\lambda}\,\kappa R\right)^{1+\vartheta}K^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}2} \  = \ D_\vartheta\,L_\vartheta R^{1+\vartheta}K^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}2} \  \le \ \Lambda_1K^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}2},
    \end{equation*}
    using $\kappa^{1+\vartheta} = \kappa_\vartheta^{1+\vartheta}L_\vartheta$ and $\tfrac{1+\lambda}{\lambda} = \tfrac52$, so that the middle term is $\left(\tfrac52\kappa_\vartheta\right)^{1+\vartheta}L_\vartheta R^{1+\vartheta}K^{-(1+\vartheta)/2} = D_\vartheta L_\vartheta R^{1+\vartheta}K^{-(1+\vartheta)/2}$ by \textup{(\ref{eq:Dtheta_Etheta})}, together with $D_\vartheta \le \tfrac{25}2$ from \Cref{lem:constants}. 
    (If $G_\star = 0$ then $\bar a = 0$ and this is the only case, so \textup{(\ref{eq:localrate})} holds with $\Lambda_2 = 0$.)

    \emph{Case 2: $A \le \bar a$} (so $G_\star > 0$).
    Split $\{0,\dots,K-1\} = \mathcal{I}\cup\mathcal{J}$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \mathcal{I} \coloneqq \{k < K:\ a_k \le \bar a\},\qquad \mathcal{J} \coloneqq \{k < K:\ a_k > \bar a\} .
    \end{equation*}
    For $k \in \mathcal{I}$, \textup{(\ref{eq:twosided})} and $\kappa a_k^{\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)} \le \kappa\bar a^{\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)} = \lambda G_\star$ give the two-sided window
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:window}
        (1-\lambda)G_\star\  \le \ {\left\| g_k \right\|}\  \le \ (1+\lambda)G_\star, \qquad\text{hence}\qquad \frac{a_k}{(1+\lambda)G_\star}\  \le \ t_k\  \le \ \frac{a_k}{(1-\lambda)G_\star}.
    \end{equation}
    For $k \in \mathcal{J}$ we have $t_k \ge h(a_k) > h(\bar a) = \bar a/\left((1+\lambda)G_\star\right)$, so $\sum_{k<K}t_k^2 \le R^2$ forces
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:Kone}
        | \mathcal{J} |\  \le \ K_1\  \coloneqq \ \frac{(1+\lambda)^2G_\star^2R^2}{\bar a^{\,2}} .
    \end{equation}

    \emph{Case 2a: $K \le 4K_1$.} Since $A \le \bar a$ we have, as in \textup{(\ref{eq:window})}, $h(A) \ge A/((1+\lambda)G_\star)$, so $K\,h(A)^2 \le R^2$ gives $A \le (1+\lambda)G_\star R\,K^{-1/2}$.
    As $\tfrac1{2-\vartheta}-\tfrac12 = \tfrac{\vartheta}{2(2-\vartheta)} \ge 0$ and $1 \le K \le 4K_1$,
    \begin{equation*}
        A\  \le \ (1+\lambda)G_\star R\,K^{-\frac1{2-\vartheta}}K^{\frac{\vartheta}{2(2-\vartheta)}} \  \le \ (1+\lambda)G_\star R\,\left(\frac{2(1+\lambda)G_\star R}{\bar a}\right)^{\frac{\vartheta}{2-\vartheta}}K^{-\frac1{2-\vartheta}},
    \end{equation*}
    using $(4K_1)^{1/2} = 2(1+\lambda)G_\star R/\bar a$.
    By \textup{(\ref{eq:abar})}, $\bar a^{-\vartheta/(2-\vartheta)} = (\kappa/(\lambda G_\star))^{(1+\vartheta)/(2-\vartheta)}$, and since $1+\tfrac{\vartheta}{2-\vartheta} = \tfrac2{2-\vartheta}$ the prefactor equals
    \begin{equation*}
        (1+\lambda)^{\frac2{2-\vartheta}}2^{\frac{\vartheta}{2-\vartheta}}\lambda^{-\frac{1+\vartheta}{2-\vartheta}} \kappa_\vartheta^{\frac{1+\vartheta}{2-\vartheta}}\, \left(G_\star^{1-\vartheta}R^2L_\vartheta\right)^{\frac1{2-\vartheta}} \  = \ E^{(1)}_\vartheta\left(L_\vartheta R^2G_\star^{1-\vartheta}\right)^{\frac1{2-\vartheta}}\  \le \ \Lambda_2 ,
    \end{equation*}
    because $(G_\star R)^{2/(2-\vartheta)}G_\star^{-(1+\vartheta)/(2-\vartheta)} = (G_\star^{1-\vartheta}R^2)^{1/(2-\vartheta)}$ and $\kappa^{(1+\vartheta)/(2-\vartheta)} = (\kappa_\vartheta^{1+\vartheta}L_\vartheta)^{1/(2-\vartheta)}$; with $\lambda = \tfrac23$ the bracket is $(\tfrac53)^2 2^\vartheta(\tfrac32)^{1+\vartheta}\kappa_\vartheta^{1+\vartheta}$.
    The final inequality holds because $E^{(1)}_\vartheta \le E_\vartheta \le 25$ by \Cref{lem:constants}, and $\Lambda_2 \ge 25\left(L_\vartheta R^2G_\star^{1-\vartheta}\right)^{1/(2-\vartheta)}$ by \textup{(\ref{eq:lambdas})}.

    \emph{Case 2b: $K > 4K_1$.} Split $\mathcal{I} = \mathcal{I}_P\cup\mathcal{I}_S$ with
    \begin{equation*}
        \mathcal{I}_P \coloneqq \{k \in \mathcal{I}:\ \rho_k \ge 2\delta_k\}\quad(\text{\emph{productive}}),\qquad \mathcal{I}_S \coloneqq \{k \in \mathcal{I}:\ \rho_k < 2\delta_k\}\quad(\text{\emph{stalling}}).
    \end{equation*}

    \emph{(i) Stalling steps are few.} For $k \in \mathcal{I}_S$, $\rho_k < 2\delta_k$ and \textup{(\ref{eq:delta_k})} give
    \begin{equation*}
        \frac{t_k^2}{\rho_k}\  > \ \frac{t_k^2}{2\delta_k}\  = \ \frac{a_k\,t_k^{1-\vartheta}}{2c} \  \ge \ \frac{a_k^{2-\vartheta}}{2c\left((1+\lambda)G_\star\right)^{1-\vartheta}} \  \ge \ \frac{A^{2-\vartheta}}{2c\left((1+\lambda)G_\star\right)^{1-\vartheta}},
    \end{equation*}
    by the left inequality of \textup{(\ref{eq:window})} and $a_k \ge A$.
    Summing and using \Cref{cor:budget},
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:NS}
        | \mathcal{I}_S |\  \le \ N\  \coloneqq \ 2c\left((1+\lambda)G_\star\right)^{1-\vartheta}R^2A^{\vartheta-2}.
    \end{equation}

    \emph{(ii) The potential.} Put $\Phi_k \coloneqq a_k^{-2}$, well defined since $a_k \ge A > 0$.
    If $k \in \mathcal{I}_P$ then $\rho_k \ge 2\delta_k$ and \textup{(\ref{eq:delta_k})} give $a_{k+1} \le a_k(1-\tfrac{\rho_k}2)$ with $1-\tfrac{\rho_k}2 \ge \tfrac12 > 0$, so, by $(1-s)^{-2} \ge 1+2s$ on $[0,1)$ applied at $s = \rho_k/2$,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:gainP}
        \Phi_{k+1}-\Phi_k\  \ge \ \Phi_k\left[(1-\tfrac{\rho_k}2)^{-2}-1\right]\  \ge \ \frac{\rho_k}{a_k^2}.
    \end{equation}
    If $k \in \mathcal{I}_S$ then $a_{k+1} \le a_k(1+\delta_k)$, so by $1-(1+s)^{-2} \le 2s$ on $[0,\infty)$ and, by the right inequality of \textup{(\ref{eq:window})}, $\delta_k = c\,t_k^{\vartheta}/{\left\| g_k \right\|} \le c\,a_k^{\vartheta}/\left((1-\lambda)^{1+\vartheta}G_\star^{1+\vartheta}\right)$,
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:lossS}
        \Phi_k-\Phi_{k+1}\  \le \ \frac{2\delta_k}{a_k^2}\  \le \ \frac{2c\,a_k^{\vartheta-2}}{(1-\lambda)^{1+\vartheta}G_\star^{1+\vartheta}} \  \le \ \frac{2c\,A^{\vartheta-2}}{(1-\lambda)^{1+\vartheta}G_\star^{1+\vartheta}},
    \end{equation}
    the last step because $\vartheta-2 < 0$ and $a_k \ge A$.
    If $k \in \mathcal{J}$ then simply $\Phi_k-\Phi_{k+1} \le \Phi_k = a_k^{-2} < \bar a^{-2}$.
    Telescoping over all $k < K$ and discarding $-\Phi_0 \le 0$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_P}\left(\Phi_{k+1}-\Phi_k\right) \  = \ \Phi_K-\Phi_0-\!\!\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_S\cup\mathcal{J}}\!\!\left(\Phi_{k+1}-\Phi_k\right) \  \le \ \Phi_K+\!\!\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_S\cup\mathcal{J}}\!\!\left(\Phi_k-\Phi_{k+1}\right),
    \end{equation*}
    so, with $\Phi_K \le A^{-2}$, \textup{(\ref{eq:gainP})}, \textup{(\ref{eq:lossS})} and \textup{(\ref{eq:Kone})},
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:potsum}
        \sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_P}\frac{\rho_k}{a_k^2}\  \le \ \frac1{A^2} \ +\ \frac{2c\,| \mathcal{I}_S |A^{\vartheta-2}}{(1-\lambda)^{1+\vartheta}G_\star^{1+\vartheta}} \ +\ \frac{K_1}{\bar a^{\,2}} .
    \end{equation}

    \emph{(iii) Productive steps are few.} By Cauchy--Schwarz, \Cref{cor:budget} and $t_k/a_k = 1/{\left\| g_k \right\|} \ge 1/\left((1+\lambda)G_\star\right)$ on $\mathcal{I}$,
    \begin{equation*}
        \left(\frac{| \mathcal{I}_P |}{(1+\lambda)G_\star}\right)^{2} \le \left(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_P}\frac{t_k}{a_k}\right)^{2} \le \left(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_P}\frac{t_k^2}{\rho_k}\right)\left(\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_P}\frac{\rho_k}{a_k^2}\right) \le R^2\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_P}\frac{\rho_k}{a_k^2}.
    \end{equation*}
    Insert \textup{(\ref{eq:potsum})} and use $\sqrt{x+y+z} \le \sqrt x+\sqrt y+\sqrt z$.
    The first term contributes $(1+\lambda)G_\star R/A$; the third contributes $(1+\lambda)G_\star R\sqrt{K_1}/\bar a = \left((1+\lambda)G_\star R/\bar a\right)^2 = K_1$; and the second, using $| \mathcal{I}_S | \le N$ from \textup{(\ref{eq:NS})}, contributes
    \begin{equation*}
        (1+\lambda)G_\star R\cdot\frac{\sqrt{2cNA^{\vartheta-2}}}{(1-\lambda)^{\frac{1+\vartheta}2}G_\star^{\frac{1+\vartheta}2}} = 2cR^2G_\star^{1-\vartheta}A^{\vartheta-2}\,\frac{(1+\lambda)^{\frac{3-\vartheta}2}}{(1-\lambda)^{\frac{1+\vartheta}2}} = \Theta\,N,\qquad \Theta \coloneqq \left(\frac{1+\lambda}{1-\lambda}\right)^{\frac{1+\vartheta}2},
    \end{equation*}
    by \textup{(\ref{eq:NS})} again.
    Altogether $| \mathcal{I}_P | \le (1+\lambda)G_\star R/A+\Theta N+K_1$, and therefore
    \begin{equation}
        \label{eq:Ksplit}
        K\  = \ | \mathcal{J} |+| \mathcal{I}_S |+| \mathcal{I}_P |\  \le \ 2K_1+(1+\Theta)N+\frac{(1+\lambda)G_\star R}{A}.
    \end{equation}

    \emph{(iv) Conclusion.} Since $K > 4K_1$ we have $2K_1 < K/2$, so \textup{(\ref{eq:Ksplit})} gives $(1+\Theta)N+(1+\lambda)G_\star R/A > K/2$ and at least one of the two terms is $\ge K/4$.

    If $(1+\Theta)N \ge K/4$ then, by \textup{(\ref{eq:NS})} and $c = L_\vartheta/(1+\vartheta)$,
    \begin{equation*}
        A^{2-\vartheta}\  \le \ \frac{8(1+\Theta)(1+\lambda)^{1-\vartheta}}{1+\vartheta}\cdot\frac{L_\vartheta R^2G_\star^{1-\vartheta}}{K}, \qquad\text{i.e.}\qquad A\  \le \ E^{(2)}_\vartheta\left(L_\vartheta R^2G_\star^{1-\vartheta}\right)^{\frac1{2-\vartheta}}K^{-\frac1{2-\vartheta}},
    \end{equation*}
    which is at most $\Lambda_2K^{-1/(2-\vartheta)}$, because $E^{(2)}_\vartheta \le E_\vartheta \le 25$ by \Cref{lem:constants}; 
    with $\lambda = \tfrac23$ one has $\Theta = 5^{(1+\vartheta)/2}$ and $1+\lambda = \tfrac53$.

    If instead $(1+\lambda)G_\star R/A \ge K/4$ then $A \le 4(1+\lambda)G_\star R\,K^{-1} \le E^{(3)}_\vartheta\,G_\star R\,K^{-1/(2-\vartheta)}$, since $1 \ge \tfrac1{2-\vartheta}$ and $K \ge 1$; this too is at most $\Lambda_2K^{-1/(2-\vartheta)}$, since $E^{(3)}_\vartheta = \tfrac{20}3 \le 25$ (\Cref{lem:constants}) and $\Lambda_2 \ge 25\,G_\star R$ by \textup{(\ref{eq:lambdas})}.

    In every case $A \le \max\{\Lambda_1K^{-(1+\vartheta)/2},\Lambda_2K^{-1/(2-\vartheta)}\}$, which is \textup{(\ref{eq:localrate})}.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
